Existence and partial regularity of suitable weak solutions to the 3D Navier-Stokes-Vlasov-Fokker-Planck equations
Renjun Duan, Fengqiang Shi, Wendong Wang, Jianbo Yu

Abstract
. In this paper, we investigate the incompressible Navier-Stokes equations coupled with the Vlasov-Fokker-Planck equation, which describes a two-phase mixture of the viscous incompressible fluid with particles or bubbles through a frictional force term. In the three-dimensional whole space, we construct a new class of suitable weak solutions to the Navier-Stokes-Vlasov-Fokker-Planck system satisfying energy estimates and three local or global energy inequalities of different forms. These obtained local energy inequalities play an important role in characterizing the measure of the singularity set of weak solutions. The main difficulties in deriving these inequalities lie in establishing the convergence of the density function f f in bounded or unbounded domains and dealing with the convergence of the non-local frictional force term. The strong convergence of both f f and f ​ log ⁡ f f\log f weighted by | v | k |v|^{k} is proved by exploring some new a priori quantities of the velocity with the help of Tao’s L p L^{p} decomposition and the DiPerna-Lions compactness method. Moreover, as an immediate consequence of the existence result, we are able to describe the Hausdorff dimension of set of singular points of the fluid velocity u u and also establish the α \alpha -Hölder continuity of f f at the regular points of u u .



2020 Mathematics Subject Classification
35Q83, 35Q84, 35Q30, 35B45

1.
Introduction

We consider the following three dimensional incompressible Navier-Stokes-Vlasov-Fokker-Planck (NSVFP) equations

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{lll}\displaystyle\partial_{t}u+(u\cdot\nabla)u-\Delta u+\nabla P-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}(v-u)fdv=0,\\[11.38109pt]
\displaystyle\partial_{t}f+(v\cdot\nabla_{x})f+\nabla_{v}\cdot\left(\left(u-v\right)f-\nabla_{v}f\right)=0,\\[8.53581pt]
\nabla\cdot u=0,\end{array}\right.

where
u=u(t,x)
with
(t,x)\in\mathbb{R}^{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}
denotes the fluid velocity field,
f=f(t,x,v)\geq 0
with
(t,x,v)\in\mathbb{R}^{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{6}_{xv}
denotes the
density distribution function of particles in the phase space and the scalar function
P
represents pressure.
The above kinetic-fluid model is widely used in the description of dynamics of the dispersed two-phase flows, bridging the macroscopic continuum mechanics and microscopic particle dynamics. Typical applications of two-phase flows can be found in
[
54
,
67
,
68
]
. The Navier-Stokes equations with random stirring forces were formally analyzed by Forster-Nelson-Stephen
[
30
]
. The long-time and long-distance properties of the
renormalized viscosity were studied by Enz
[
28
]
. Moreover, there exist many variants of kinetic-fluid models depending on the different physical regimes, such as the compressibility of the fluid, viscosity of the fluid, species
of particles, interactions between the fluid and particles, and
so on. Below we recall some kinetic-fluid models related to system (
1
) under consideration.

The nonlinear Fokker-Planck equation.
Constantin in
[
23
]
considered the nonlinear Fokker-Planck equation coupled with the Stokes system and proved the existence of smooth solutions
for a wide class of such systems of type II. By assuming that the added stress tensor is given and satisfies suitable properties, Constantin-Fefferman-Titi-Zarnescu
[
21
]
proved the regularity of Navier-Stokes-Fokker-Planck system in 2D. Global regularity was first proved by Constantin-Masmoudi in
[
22
]
. Independently and
simultaneously, global regularity for a similar model was proved by Lin-Zhang-Zhang in
[
46
]
. In that work, the model is a version of the FENE model in which the physical gradient of
velocity is replaced by its anti-symmetric part, and the particles are restricted
to the unit disk by a potential that is infinite at the unit circle.
Constantin-Seregin in
[
24
]
gave a quantitative bounds in the two-dimensional torus domain
\mathbb{T}^{2}
. Choi-Jeong-Kang proved the global existence, uniqueness, and decay of solutions near equilibrium for the Vlasov-Riesz-Fokker-Planck system with small, regular initial data
[
18
]
. For more references, we refer to
[
23
,
3
,
4
,
12
]
and the references therein.

The incompressible Navier-Stokes equations coupled with linear
Fokker-Planck equations.

The mathematical
analysis of incompressible kinetic-fluid models has received much attention recently.
Hamdache
[
35
]
analyzed the Vlasov-Stokes system in a bounded domain and constructed weak solutions under specular reflection boundary conditions in 1998. In 2007, Lin-Liu-Zhang established the global existence and uniqueness of classical solutions to the micro-macro model in
[
44
]
. Later, in 2007, Otto-Tzavaras
[
53
]
showed that for the Doi model describing suspensions of rod-like molecules in the dilute regime, discontinuities in the velocity gradient cannot form in finite time, even without a microscopic cut-off. Goudon-He-Moussa-Zhang
[
33
]
established the global existence of
classical solutions near the equilibrium for the incompressible Navier-Stokes-Vlasov-Fokker-Planck system; meanwhile, Carrillo-Duan-Moussa
[
13
]
studied the corresponding
inviscid case. Subsequently, in 2011, Chae-Kang-Lee
[
14
]
established the global existence of weak solutions in two and three dimensions, as well as the existence and uniqueness of global smooth solutions in two dimensions. In 2017, Boudin et al.
[
8
]
demonstrated the global existence of weak solutions for the three-dimensional incompressible Vlasov-Navier-Stokes equations, where the coupling is through a drag force depending linearly on the relative velocity between the fluid and the particles. Su-Yao
[
60
]
considered the hydrodynamic limit for the inhomogeneous
incompressible Navier-Stokes-Vlasov-Fokker-Planck equations in a two or three dimensional bounded domain when the initial density is bounded away from zero. More recently, in 2024, Nfor-Woukeng
[
52
]
proved the existence of a unique weak probabilistic solution for a stochastic Navier-Stokes-Vlasov-Fokker-Planck system, and Jin-Lin
[
36
]
established exponential decay of energy over time by using hypocoercivity arguments in a multi-phase Navier-Stokes-Vlasov-Fokker-Planck system. For more references, we refer to
[
33
]
and the references therein.

The compressible Navier-Stokes equations coupled with linear
Fokker-Planck equations.

Mellet-Vasseur
[
50
]
proved the global existence of weak solutions to the compressible Vlasov-Fokker-Planck-Navier-Stokes equations with either absorption or reflection boundary conditions. In the same year, they also studied certain asymptotic regimes of the system
[
49
]
. In
[
51
]

they further studied the asymptotic analysis of the solutions. In 2006, Baranger-Desvillettes
[
1
]
established the local existence of solutions to the compressible Vlasov-Euler equations. Later, in 2017, Li-Mu-Wang
[
43
]
proved the global well-posedness of strong solutions to the compressible Navier-Stokes-Vlasov-Fokker-Planck equations in the three-dimensional whole space. In 2021, Choi-Jung
[
20
]
obtained the global-in-time existence of weak solutions to the coupled kinetic-fluid system, as well as the existence and uniqueness of strong solutions to the limiting system in a bounded domain, equipped with kinematic boundary conditions for the Euler part and Dirichlet conditions for the Navier-Stokes part.
In
[
41
]
Li-Liu-Yang proved the global existence of unique strong
solution and its exponential convergence rate to the equilibrium state under
the Maxwell boundary condition for the incompressible case and specular reflection
boundary condition for the compressible case, respectively. The existence, uniqueness, and regularity of global weak solution to the
initial value problem for general initial data ware established by Li-Shou
[
42
]
in one dimensional spatial periodic domain.
More recently, in 2023, Chen-Li-Li-Zamponi
[
15
]
established the global existence of weak solutions for the compressible Vlasov-Poisson-Fokker-Planck-Navier-Stokes equations in a three-dimensional bounded domain, under nonhomogeneous Dirichlet boundary conditions and allowing arbitrarily large initial and boundary data, provided the adiabatic exponent satisfies
\gamma>\frac{3}{2}
. More related results can be found in
[
2
,
1
,
31
,
48
,
61
,
27
,
19
]
, and so on.

The incompressible Navier-Stokes equations.
When
f=0
, the system (
1
) reduces to the Navier-Stokes equations. It is well known that the Navier-Stokes equations describe the macroscopic motion of an incompressible viscous fluid:

\displaystyle\partial_{t}u+\left(u\cdot\nabla\right)u-\nu\Delta u+\nabla P=0,

\displaystyle\nabla\cdot u=0,

where
u\left(t,x\right)
is the velocity field,
\nu
is the kinematic viscosity, and
P\left(t,x\right)
is the pressure. While the Navier-Stokes equations effectively describe continuum fluid flows, they lack the resolution to capture the behavior of individual particles suspended in the medium.
The study of partial regularity and suitable weak solutions in PDEs is essential, because many nonlinear equations arising in physics and geometry, such as the Navier-Stokes equations or harmonic map flows, do not admit globally smooth solutions. Instead, their solutions may develop singularities, yet often retain regularity outside a small, lower-dimensional set. Weak solutions provide a rigorous framework to analyze such equations, while partial regularity theory quantifies where and how smoothness persists, offering critical insights into the structure of singularities and their physical relevance. This approach bridges theoretical PDE analysis with applied problems, ensuring meaningful interpretations even in the presence of irregular behavior. The concept of suitable weak solutions for the Navier-Stokes equations was initially introduced by Scheffer in
[
57
,
55
,
56
]
. Later, Caffarelli-Kohn-Nirenberg
[
9
]
established that the set
S
of possible interior singular points for a suitable weak solution has a parabolic Hausdorff dimension of zero. Compared to the Leray-Hopf weak solution proposed by Leray in
[
40
]
, the suitable weak solution exhibits improved properties. For instance, if a local strong solution loses regularity and blows up, it can still be continued as a suitable weak solution (see Proposition 30.1 in
[
38
]
). For simplified proofs and further advancements, we refer readers to the works of Lin
[
45
]
, Ladyzhenskaya-Seregin
[
37
]
, Tian and Xin
[
64
]
, Seregin
[
58
]
, Gustafson-Kang-Tsai
[
34
]
, Vasseur
[
66
]
, and related references. Recently, Chen-Li-Wang-Wang proved global existence of suitable weak solutions to the 3D chemotaxis-Navier-Stokes equations in
[
16
]
and derive the Hausdorff measure for the singularity set in
[
17
]
.

Motivated by
[
9
]
and
[
17
]
, we study the partial regularity of the system (
1
). In what follows we denote
L^{2}_{\sigma}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x})=\{u\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x})|\nabla\cdot u=0\}
and
\dot{H}^{2}_{\sigma}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x})=\{u\in\dot{H}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x})|\nabla\cdot u=0\}
.

Definition 1.1
.

A pair
\left(u,f\right)
is called a suitable weak solution of the system (
1
) with initial data
\left(u_{in},f_{in}\right)
satisfying condition (
1.5
) to be specified later, if all the following properties hold:

\begin{aligned} \text{(i).}\ &u\in L^{\infty}_{loc}\left([0,+\infty);L^{2}_{\sigma}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x})\right)\cap L^{2}_{loc}\left([0,+\infty);\dot{H}^{1}_{\sigma}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x})\right),\\
&f(t,x,v)\geq 0,f\in L^{\infty}_{loc}\left([0,+\infty);L^{\infty}\cap L^{1}\left(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}\right)\right),\\
&\left(|x|^{2}+|v|^{2}\right)f\in L^{\infty}_{loc}\left([0,+\infty);L^{1}\left(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}\right)\right),\\
&f\log f\in L^{\infty}_{loc}\left([0,+\infty);L^{1}\left(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}\right)\right),\\
&P\in L^{\frac{5}{3}}_{loc}\left([0,+\infty);L^{\frac{5}{3}}\left(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\right)\right);\end{aligned}

\begin{aligned} \text{(ii).}\ &u\in L^{1}_{loc}\left([0,+\infty);L^{p}_{\sigma}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x})\right)~\left(p\in\left[2,+\infty\right)\right),\\
&|v|^{\kappa}f\in L^{\infty}_{loc}\left([0,+\infty);L^{1}\left(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}\right)\right)~\left(\kappa\in\left[3,+\infty\right)\right),\\
&|v|^{\bar{\kappa}}f\log f\in L^{\infty}_{loc}\left([0,+\infty);L^{\eta}\left(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}\right)\right)~\left(\forall~\bar{\kappa}\in\left[0,\kappa\right),\eta>1~\text{s.t.}~\eta\bar{\kappa}\leq\kappa;\kappa\in\left[3,+\infty\right)\right),\\
&|v|^{\frac{\bar{\kappa}}{2}}f\log f\in{L^{1}((0,T)\times\Omega_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})}~\left(\forall~\bar{\kappa}\in\left[0,\kappa\right);\kappa\in\left[3,+\infty\right)\right);\end{aligned}

(iii).
\left(u,f\right)
solves (
1
) in the sense of distributions;

(iv).
\left(u,f\right)
satisfies the energy inequality

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}|u(t,\cdot)|^{2}dx+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{6}_{xv}}\left(\frac{1}{2}|v|^{2}f+f\log f\right)(t,\cdot)dxdv

\displaystyle+\int_{(0,t)\times\mathbb{R}_{x}^{3}}|\nabla u|^{2}dxds+\int_{(0,t)\times\mathbb{R}_{xv}^{6}}\frac{\left|(u-v)f-\nabla_{v}f\right|^{2}}{f}dxdvds

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{6}_{xv}}\left(\frac{1}{2}|v|^{2}f_{in}+f_{in}\log f_{in}\right)dxdv+\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}|u_{in}|^{2}dx;

(v).
\left(u,f\right)
satisfies the first type of local energy inequality as

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega_{x}}\left(|u|^{2}\psi\right)(t,\cdot)dx+\int_{\Omega_{x}\times\Omega_{v}}\left(\left(\frac{1}{2}|v|^{2}f+f\log f\right)\phi\right)(t,\cdot)dxdv+\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega_{x}}|\nabla u|^{2}\psi dxds

\displaystyle+\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega_{x}\times\Omega_{v}}\frac{|(u-v)f-\nabla_{v}f|^{2}}{f}\phi dxdvds\leq\frac{1}{2}\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega_{x}}|u|^{2}(\partial_{t}\psi+\Delta_{x}\psi)dxds

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega_{x}}|u|^{2}u\cdot\nabla_{x}\psi dxds+\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega_{x}\times\Omega_{v}}\left(\frac{|v|^{2}}{2}+\log f\right)f(\partial_{t}\phi+\Delta_{v}\phi)dxdvds

\displaystyle+\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega_{x}\times\Omega_{v}}\left(\frac{|v|^{2}}{2}+\log f\right)fv\cdot\left(\nabla_{x}\phi-\nabla_{v}\phi\right)dxdvds+\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega_{x}}(P-\bar{P})u\cdot\nabla_{x}\psi dxds

\displaystyle-\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega_{x}\times\Omega_{v}}fv\cdot u\phi dxdvds+\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}fv\cdot u\psi dxdvds

\displaystyle+\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega_{x}\times\Omega_{v}}\left(2+\frac{|v|^{2}}{2}+\log f\right)fu\cdot\nabla_{v}\phi dxdvds,

where
\phi,\psi\geq 0
and they vanish in the parabolic boundary of
\left(0,t\right)\times\Omega_{x}\times\Omega_{v}
and
\left(0,t\right)\times\Omega_{x}
, respectively. Here,
\Omega_{x}
and
\Omega_{v}
are two bounded domains in
\mathbb{R}^{3}
. Here and in the sequel, we denote
\bar{P}:=\frac{1}{|\Omega_{x}|}\int_{\Omega_{x}}Pdx
.

(vi). When
\kappa>3
,
\left(u,f\right)
satisfies the second type of local energy inequality as

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega_{x}}\left(|u|^{2}\psi\right)(t,\cdot)dx+\int_{\Omega_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\left(\left(\frac{1}{2}|v|^{2}f+f\log f\right)\psi\right)(t,\cdot)dxdv+\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega_{x}}|\nabla u|^{2}\psi dxds

\displaystyle+\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\frac{|(u-v)f-\nabla_{v}f|^{2}}{f}\psi dxdvds\leq\frac{1}{2}\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega_{x}}|u|^{2}(\partial_{t}\psi+\Delta_{x}\psi)dxds

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega_{x}}|u|^{2}u\cdot\nabla_{x}\psi dx+\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\left(\frac{|v|^{2}}{2}+\log f\right)f\partial_{t}\psi dxdvds

\displaystyle+\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\left(\frac{|v|^{2}}{2}+\log f\right)fv\cdot\nabla_{x}\psi dxdvds+\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega_{x}}(P-\bar{P})u\cdot\nabla_{x}\psi dxds.

In the following, the moments of
f(t,x,v)
are denoted

\displaystyle m_{\alpha}f(t,x)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}_{v}^{3}}f(t,x,v)|v|^{\alpha}dv,

\displaystyle M_{\alpha}f(t)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}_{xv}^{6}}f(t,x,v)|v|^{\alpha}dxdv,

for any
t\in[0,T]
and
\alpha\geq 0
. Note that

\displaystyle M_{\alpha}f(t)=\int_{\mathbb{R}_{x}^{3}}m_{\alpha}f(t,x)dx.

Our first main theorem is stated as follows.

Theorem 1.2
.

Assume that the initial data
\left(u_{in},f_{in}\right)
satisfies

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}u_{in}\in L^{2}_{\sigma}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x});\\
f_{in}\in L^{\infty}\cap L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{6}_{xv}),~f_{in}\geq 0;\\
\left(|x|^{2}+|v|^{\kappa}+|\log f_{in}|\right)f_{in}\in L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{6}_{xv}),~\kappa\geq 3.\end{array}\right.

(1.5)

Then, for the Cauchy problem on the system (
1
) with
\left.(u,f)\right|_{t=0}=\left(u_{in},f_{in}\right)
, there exists a global suitable weak solution in the sense of Definition
1.1
.

Remark 1.3
.

Compared with the usual definition of weak solutions, these properties
(ii),(v),(vi)
in Definition
1.1
are completely new. It seems difficult to derive the uniform bound on
|v|^{m}f
for
m>3
due to the whole space by Lemma
2.2
, although one can assume some regular initial data (see the details in Section 3.2), where the large
L^{p}
norm in the space is necessary for the velocity. With the help of Tao’s Littlewood-Paley decomposition (cf.
[
62
]
) to the Navier-Stokes equations, the a priori estimates of
\|u\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{p}_{x}}
are obtained, which implies the
L^{\infty}_{t}L^{1}_{xv}
norm of
|v|^{m}f
is uniformly bounded (see Lemma
3.6
and
3.7
for mare details). Combining these estimates and DiPerna-Lions compactness method in
[
25
]
, the strong convergence of
f
,
|v|^{m}f
and
|v|^{m}f\log f
are obtained (see Section 5.2, 5.3, 5.5), which plays an important role in the local energy inequalities of
(v),(vi)
. In order to prove
(v),(vi)
, we also have used Lions’ weak convergence method in
[
47
]
(see Section 5.1 for details) and Aubin-Lions lemma for the strong convergence of the velocity (see Section 5.4).

The proof of Theorem
1.2
is based on the global well-posedness for the following regularized system:

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{llll}\displaystyle\partial_{t}u^{\varepsilon,\delta}+(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta})\cdot\nabla u^{\varepsilon,\delta}-\Delta u^{\varepsilon,\delta}+\nabla_{x}P^{\varepsilon,\delta}-\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast\left\{\int_{\mathbb{R}_{v}^{3}}(v-\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta})f^{\varepsilon,\delta}dv\right\}=0,\\
\displaystyle\partial_{t}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}+(v\cdot\nabla_{x})f^{\varepsilon,\delta}+\nabla_{v}\cdot[(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta}-v\gamma_{\delta}(v))f^{\varepsilon,\delta}-\nabla_{v}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}]=0,\\
\displaystyle\nabla_{x}\cdot u^{\varepsilon,\delta}=0,\\
\displaystyle f^{\varepsilon,\delta}(0,x,v)=f_{in}^{\delta}(x,v),\quad u^{\varepsilon,\delta}(0,x)=u_{in}^{\delta}(x).\end{array}\right.

Here, for
\varepsilon>0
, we define
\theta_{\varepsilon}
=
\varepsilon^{-3}\theta(\frac{x}{\varepsilon})
with
\theta\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R}_{x}^{3})
,
\theta\geq 0
, and
\int_{\mathbb{R}_{x}^{3}}\theta=1
. Moreover, for
\delta>0
we introduce
\gamma_{\delta}\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}_{v}^{3})
whose support is included in the ball
B(0,\frac{1}{\delta})
such that
0\leq\gamma_{\delta}\leq 1,\gamma_{\delta}=1

on
B(0,\frac{1}{2\delta})
,
v\cdot\nabla_{v}\gamma_{\delta}(v)\leq 0
, and
|v\cdot\nabla_{v}\gamma_{\delta}(v)|\leq C
.
Besides, assume that

\displaystyle u_{in}^{\delta}(x):=\theta_{\delta}\ast u_{in},\quad\delta>0,

(1.11)

where
\theta
is as above,
and

\displaystyle f_{in}^{\delta}(x,v):=\varrho_{\{|v|\leq\frac{1}{\delta}\}}f_{in},

(1.12)

where
\varrho_{\{|v|\leq\frac{1}{\delta}\}}=1
for
|v|\leq\frac{1}{\delta}
and
=0
otherwise.

For (
1
), we have the following global well-posedness result.

Theorem 1.4
.

Let
f_{in},u_{in}
be assumed as in Theorem
1.2
. Then there exists a global strong solution
\left(u^{\varepsilon,\delta},f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right)
of the regularized problem (
1
) with the initial data (
1.11
) and (
1.12
), which satisfies

(i)

\displaystyle 0\leq f^{\varepsilon,\delta}

\displaystyle\in L^{\infty}\left((0,T);L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{6}_{xv})\right)\cap L^{\infty}\left((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{6}_{xv}\right);

\displaystyle u^{\varepsilon,\delta}

\displaystyle\in L^{\infty}\left((0,T);L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x})\right)\cap L^{\infty}\left((0,T);H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x})\right);

\displaystyle|v|^{2}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}

\displaystyle\in L^{\infty}\left((0,T);L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{6}_{xv})\right)

and

\displaystyle\|u^{\varepsilon,\delta}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}(0,T;{L^{2}_{x}})}+\left\||v|^{2}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;{L^{1}_{xv}})}+\|\nabla u^{\varepsilon,\delta}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(0,T;{L^{2}_{x}})}+\|\nabla u^{\varepsilon,\delta}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}(0,T;{L^{2}_{x}})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\left(\varepsilon,\delta,\|f_{in}\|_{L^{\infty}_{xv}},\|f_{in}\|_{L^{1}_{xv}},\left\||v|^{2}f_{in}^{\delta}\right\|_{L^{1}_{xv}},\|u_{in}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{2}\right)\left(T+1\right)^{5}e^{C(\varepsilon)(T+1)^{2}e^{2T}}.

(ii)
\left\|f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{1}_{xv}}\leq\left\|f_{in}\right\|_{L^{1}_{xv}}
,
\left\|f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{txv}}\leq e^{3T}\left\|f_{in}\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{xv}}
.

Moreover, as an immediate consequence of Theorem
1.2
together with Lemma
A.8
and Lemma
A.7
in the appendix, we are able to describe the Hausdorff dimension of set of singular points of the fluid velocity
u
, and also establish the
\alpha
-Hölder continuity of
f
at the regular points of
u
. The main results are stated as follows.

Theorem 1.5
.

Let all the conditions of Theorem
1.2
be satisfied. It is further assumed that

\displaystyle|v|^{15+\varkappa}f_{in}\in L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{6}_{xv})

(1.13)

with
\varkappa>0
. Then, there is a small
\varepsilon_{0}>0
such that if
u
satisfies

\displaystyle\limsup_{r\to 0+}\frac{1}{r}\iint_{Q(z_{0},r)}|\nabla u|^{2}<\varepsilon_{0},

where
Q(z_{0},r):=(t_{0}-r^{2},t_{0})\times B(x_{0},r)
for
z_{0}=(t_{0},x_{0})
, then
u
is regular at
z_{0}
in the sense that there exists
r>0
such that
u\in L^{\infty}(Q(z_{0},r))
.
Moreover, it holds

\displaystyle P^{1}(S_{u,sing})=0,

where

\displaystyle S_{u,sing}=\{z\in(0,T]\times\Omega_{x}:u\text{ is not regular at }z\}.

Theorem 1.6
.

Let all the conditions of Theorem
1.2
together with (
1.13
) be satisfied. Given
(0,T]\times\Omega_{x}\times\Omega_{v}\subset(0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}
, let

\displaystyle S_{u,reg}=\{z\in(0,T]\times\Omega_{x}:u\text{ is regular at }z\},

then for any
z_{0}\in S_{u,reg}
, the solution
f(t,x,v)
of (
1
) is
\alpha
-Hölder continuous
in
Q_{\text{int}}
satisfying

\displaystyle\|f\|_{C^{\alpha}(Q_{\text{int}})}\leq C\left(\|f\|_{L^{2}(Q_{\text{ext}})}+\|f\|_{L^{\infty}(Q_{\text{ext}})}\right),

with constants
\alpha=\alpha(\Lambda)\in(0,1)
and
C=C(\Lambda,Q_{\text{ext}},Q_{\text{int}})
, where
Q_{\text{ext}}:=(t_{0}-r^{2},t_{0})\times B(x_{0},r)\times B(v,r)
and
Q_{\text{int}}:=(t_{0}-r_{1}^{2},t_{0})\times B(x_{0},r_{1})\times B(v,r_{1})
with
r_{1}<r_{0}
.

Remark 1.7
.

To the best of our knowledge, this is the first result establishing the existence of
suitable weak solutions
and quantifying the Hausdorff dimension of the singular set for the full 3D NSVFP system. The requirement of moments up to order
15+\kappa
in (
1.13
) is technical but necessary for our analysis. It ensures that the frictional force term
\int(v-u)fdv
belongs to
L^{q}_{t,x}
with
q>5/2
. This specific threshold allows us to treat the coupling term as a perturbation that does not deteriorate the parabolic Hausdorff dimension of the singular set (see Lemma
A.7
and Lemma
A.8
).

The rest of the paper is organized as follows. Section 2 is devoted to proving Theorem
1.4
on the existence of global solutions for the regularized system. The proof of Theorem
1.2
is stated in Section 3-8. In Section 3 we show the uniform estimates independent of
\varepsilon,\delta
for the regularized system. In Section 4, we obtain the global energy inequality, local energy inequality in the first and second types. Next, we derive in Section 5 the convergence of
u^{\varepsilon,\delta}
,
f^{\varepsilon,\delta}
, and
f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\log f^{\varepsilon,\delta}
, which are essential for the energy estimates. In Section 6, Section 7 and Section 8 the convergence of the three energy inequalities are proved, respectively. The proof of Theorem
1.5
and Theorem
1.6
is given briefly in Section 9. Some technical lemmas are added in the Appendix.

2.
Global existence of strong solutions for the regularized system

In this section, we construct global solutions of the regularized system (
1
), which is divided into two subsections.

2.1.
Local existence of regularized solution

In this subsection, we first construct local solutions of the regularized system (
1
) by the fundamental solutions of Stokes and Fokker-Planck equations and Banach fixed-point theorem.

2.1.1.
The construction of a mapping

Let
T\in(0,1)
and

\displaystyle R=2\left(\|u_{in}^{\delta}\|_{H^{1}_{x}}+\|f_{in}^{\delta}\|_{L^{1}_{xv}}+\|f_{in}^{\delta}\|_{L^{\infty}_{xv}}+\left\||v|^{2}f_{in}^{\delta}\right\|_{L^{1}_{xv}}\right)<\infty.

Then we introduce the following Banach space:

X:=L^{\infty}\left(0,T;W^{1,2}_{\sigma}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\times[{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3})\cap L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3})}]\times L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3})\right)

along with its closed subset

\Gamma:=\bigg\{(u^{\varepsilon,\delta},f^{\varepsilon,\delta},|v|^{2}f^{\varepsilon,\delta})\in X\mid\left\|(u^{\varepsilon,\delta},f^{\varepsilon,\delta},|v|^{2}f^{\varepsilon,\delta})\right\|_{\Gamma}\leq R\bigg\},

where

\displaystyle\left\|(u^{\varepsilon,\delta},f^{\varepsilon,\delta},|v|^{2}f^{\varepsilon,\delta})\right\|_{\Gamma}:=

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in(0,T)}\|u^{\varepsilon,\delta}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\sup_{t\in(0,T)}\|\nabla u^{\varepsilon,\delta}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}

\displaystyle+\sup_{t\in(0,T)}\|f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\|_{L^{1}_{xv}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\sup_{t\in(0,T)}\|f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\|_{L^{\infty}_{xv}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3})}

\displaystyle+\sup_{t\in(0,T)}\left\||v|^{2}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right\|_{L^{1}_{xv}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3})}.

For
(u^{\varepsilon,\delta},f^{\varepsilon,\delta},|v|^{2}f^{\varepsilon,\delta})\in\Gamma
and
t\in(0,T)
, let

\displaystyle\Phi(u^{\varepsilon,\delta},f^{\varepsilon,\delta},|v|^{2}f^{\varepsilon,\delta})

\displaystyle=\left(\begin{array}[]{llll}\Phi_{1}(u^{\varepsilon,\delta},f^{\varepsilon,\delta},|v|^{2}f^{\varepsilon,\delta})(t,x)\\
\Phi_{2}(u^{\varepsilon,\delta},f^{\varepsilon,\delta},|v|^{2}f^{\varepsilon,\delta})(t,x,v)\\
\Phi_{3}(u^{\varepsilon,\delta},f^{\varepsilon,\delta},|v|^{2}f^{\varepsilon,\delta})(t,x,v)\end{array}\right)

\displaystyle:=\left(\begin{array}[]{llll}e^{t\triangle}u_{in}^{\delta}-\int^{t}_{0}e^{(t-\tau)\triangle}\mathbb{P}\bigg\{(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta})\cdot\nabla u^{\varepsilon,\delta}+\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast\left\{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(v-\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta})f^{\varepsilon,\delta}dv\right\}\bigg\}d\tau\\
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}G(t,x,v;0,\xi,\rho)f_{in}^{\delta}(\xi,\rho)d\xi d\rho\\
\hskip 28.45274pt-\int^{t}_{0}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}G(t,x,v;\tau,\xi,\rho)\nabla_{\rho}\cdot\left[\left(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta}-\rho\gamma_{\delta}(\rho)\right)f^{\varepsilon,\delta}(\xi,\rho)\right]d\xi d\rho d\tau\\
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}G(t,x,v;0,\xi,\rho)h_{in}^{\delta}(\xi,\rho)d\xi d\rho\\
\hskip 28.45274pt-\int^{t}_{0}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}G(t,x,v;\tau,\xi,\rho)\bigg\{\nabla_{\rho}\cdot\left[\left(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta}-\rho\gamma_{\delta}(\rho)\right)h^{\varepsilon,\delta}(\xi,\rho)\right]\\
\hskip 34.14322pt-2\rho\cdot\left[\left(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta}-\rho\gamma_{\delta}(\rho)\right)f^{\varepsilon,\delta}(\xi,\rho)\right]+6f^{\epsilon,\delta}+4\rho\cdot\nabla_{\rho}f^{\epsilon,\delta}\bigg\}d\xi d\rho d\tau\end{array}\right),

where

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} G(t,x,v;\tau,\xi,\rho)&=G(t-\tau,\rho,v,x-\xi)\\
&=\frac{3^{\frac{3}{2}}}{(2\pi)^{3}(t-\tau)^{6}}exp\left\{-\frac{3\big|x-\xi-[\frac{t-\tau}{2}](\rho+v)\big|^{2}}{(t-\tau)^{3}}-\frac{|\rho-v|^{2}}{4(t-\tau)}\right\}\end{aligned}

is the fundamental solution ( see, for example,
[
10
]
) of

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\partial_{t}g-\triangle_{v}g+v\cdot\nabla_{x}g=0,&\quad\text{in }\mathbb{R}^{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v},\\
g(0,x,v)=g_{0}(x,v),&\quad\text{in }\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}.\end{array}\right.

(2.16)

The family of operators
(e^{t\triangle})_{t\geq 0}
denotes the heat semigroup, and
\mathbb{P}
represents the Leray projection on
L^{2}
. The terms
h^{\varepsilon,\delta}
and

h_{in}^{\delta}
correspond to
|v|^{2}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}
and
|v|^{2}f_{in}^{\delta}
, respectively.

Additionally, since
G
is a fundamental solution in (
2.16
), we give some known properties in the following:

Lemma 2.1

(Lemma 1 in
[
10
]
)
.

(i) For some positive constants
m_{1},m_{2},M_{1},M_{2}
, there hold

\begin{array}[]{l}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}G(t,x,v;\tau,\xi,\rho)\,dv=\frac{M_{1}}{(t-\tau)^{\frac{9}{2}}}\exp\left\{-\frac{|x-\xi-(t-\tau)\rho|^{2}}{m_{1}(t-\tau)^{3}}\right\},\\
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}G(t,x,v;\tau,\xi,\rho)\,dx=\frac{M_{2}}{(t-\tau)^{\frac{3}{2}}}\exp\left\{-\frac{|\rho-v|^{2}}{m_{2}(t-\tau)}\right\},\\
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{6}}G(t,x,v;\tau,\xi,\rho)\,dxdv=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{6}}G(t,x,v;\tau,\xi,\rho)\,d\xi d\rho=1,\end{array}

with
x,v,\xi,\rho\in\mathbb{R}^{3}
and
t>\tau\geq 0
.

(ii) The following estimates hold for some positive constant
C
:

\begin{array}[]{l}|\nabla_{v}G(t,x,v;\tau,\xi,\rho)|\leq C\frac{G(t,x/2,v/2;\tau,\xi/2,\rho/2)}{(t-\tau)^{\frac{1}{2}}},\\
|\nabla_{\rho}G(t,x,v;\tau,\xi,\rho)|\leq C\frac{G(t,x/2,v/2;\tau,\xi/2,\rho/2)}{(t-\tau)^{\frac{1}{2}}}.\end{array}

Lemma 2.2

(Lemma 4.2 in
[
11
]
)
.

Let
\beta>0
and
f
be a nonnegative function in
L^{\infty}\left(\left(0,T\right)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\right)
, such that
m_{\beta}f(t,x)<+\infty
, for a.e. (t, x). The following estimate
holds for any
\alpha<\beta
(Lemma 1 in
[
7
]
):

\displaystyle m_{\alpha}f(t,x)\leq\left(\frac{4}{3}\pi\|f(t,x,v)\|_{L^{\infty}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3})}+1\right)\left(m_{\beta}f(t,x)\right)^{\frac{\alpha+3}{\beta+3}},\quad a.e.\ (t,x).

(2.17)

Moreover, the following inequalities hold:

\displaystyle\left\||v|^{\mu}f\right\|_{L^{1}_{xv}}\leq\|f\|_{L^{1}_{xv}}^{1-\frac{\mu}{\lambda}}\left\||v|^{\lambda}f\right\|^{\frac{\mu}{\lambda}}_{L^{1}_{xv}},

\displaystyle\lambda>\mu>0,

(2.18)

\displaystyle\left\|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|v|^{\mu}fdv\right\|_{L_{x}^{\frac{3+\lambda}{3+\mu}}}\leq C_{\lambda,\mu}\|f\|^{\frac{\lambda-\mu}{3+\lambda}}_{L^{\infty}_{xv}}\left\||v|^{\lambda}f\right\|^{\frac{3+\mu}{3+\lambda}}_{L^{1}_{xv}},

\displaystyle\lambda>\mu>0.

(2.19)

2.1.2.
\Phi
is a mapping from
\Gamma
to
\Gamma

We make estimates on each part as follows.

Estimation of
\Phi_{1}
.
By Lemma
A.2
, we get

\displaystyle\|\Phi_{1}(t,\cdot)\|_{L_{x}^{2}}\leq

\displaystyle\|u_{in}^{\delta}\|_{L_{x}^{2}}+\int^{t}_{0}\left\|e^{(t-\tau)\triangle}\mathbb{P}\left(({\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta})\cdot\nabla u^{\varepsilon,\delta}}\right)\right\|_{L_{x}^{2}}d\tau

\displaystyle+\int^{t}_{0}\left\|e^{(t-\tau)\triangle}\mathbb{P}\left\{\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}vf^{\varepsilon,\delta}dv\right\}\right\|_{L_{x}^{2}}d\tau

\displaystyle+\int^{t}_{0}\left\|e^{(t-\tau)\triangle}\mathbb{P}\left\{\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\epsilon,\delta})f^{\varepsilon,\delta}dv\right\}\right\|_{L_{x}^{2}}d\tau

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\|u_{in}^{\delta}\|_{L_{x}^{2}}+\sum_{i=1}^{3}A_{1i}.

For
A_{11}
, by using Hölder’s inequality and Lemma
A.2
we have

\displaystyle A_{11}

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{0}^{t}(t-\tau)^{-\frac{3}{4}}d\tau\|(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta})\cdot\nabla u^{\varepsilon,\delta}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}))}

\displaystyle\leq CT^{\frac{1}{4}}\|u^{\varepsilon,\delta}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}))}\|\nabla u^{\varepsilon,\delta}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}))}.

For
A_{12}
, it follows from Lemma
2.2
that

\displaystyle A_{12}

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{0}^{t}(t-\tau)^{-\frac{9}{20}}d\tau\big\|m_{1}|f^{\varepsilon,\delta}|\big\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{\frac{5}{4}}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}))}

\displaystyle\leq CT^{\frac{11}{20}}\left(\frac{4}{3}\pi\|f^{\varepsilon,\delta}(t,x,v)\|_{L^{\infty}_{txv}}+1\right)\left\||v|^{2}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{6}_{xv}))}^{\frac{4}{5}}.

For
A_{13}
, using Lemma
2.2
again we derive

\displaystyle A_{13}

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{0}^{t}(t-\tau)^{-\frac{3}{4}}d\tau\left\||\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta}|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|f^{\varepsilon,\delta}|dv\right\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}))}

\displaystyle\leq CT^{\frac{1}{4}}\left(\frac{4}{3}\pi\|f^{\varepsilon,\delta}(t,x,v)\|_{L^{\infty}_{txv}}+1\right)\left\||\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta}|\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|v|^{2}|f^{\varepsilon,\delta}|dv\right)^{\frac{3}{5}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}))}

\displaystyle\leq CT^{\frac{1}{4}}\left(\frac{4}{3}\pi\|f^{\varepsilon,\delta}(t,x,v)\|_{L^{\infty}_{txv}}+1\right)\|\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{\frac{5}{2}}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}))}\left\||v|^{2}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{6}_{xv}))}^{\frac{3}{5}}

\displaystyle\leq CT^{\frac{1}{4}}\left(\frac{4}{3}\pi\|f^{\varepsilon,\delta}(t,x,v)\|_{L^{\infty}_{txv}}+1\right)\left\||v|^{2}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{6}_{xv}))}^{\frac{3}{5}}\times

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\left(\|u^{\varepsilon,\delta}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}))}+\|\nabla u^{\varepsilon,\delta}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}))}\right).

Due to
T\in(0,1)
, we derive

\displaystyle\|\Phi_{1}(t,\cdot)\|_{L_{x}^{2}}\leq

\displaystyle\|u_{in}^{\delta}\|_{L_{x}^{2}}+C(R)T^{\frac{1}{4}}\quad\text{for all}~t\in(0,T).

By similar arguments as in
\|\Phi_{1}\|_{L_{x}^{2}}
, we get

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{x}\Phi_{1}(t,\cdot)\|_{L_{x}^{2}}\leq

\displaystyle\|\nabla u_{in}^{\delta}\|_{L_{x}^{2}}+\int^{t}_{0}\left\|\nabla_{x}e^{(t-\tau)\triangle}\mathbb{P}\left(({\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta})\cdot\nabla u^{\varepsilon,\delta}}\right)\right\|_{L_{x}^{2}}d\tau

\displaystyle+\int^{t}_{0}\left\|\nabla_{x}e^{(t-\tau)\triangle}\mathbb{P}\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast\left\{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}vf^{\varepsilon,\delta}dv\right\}\right\|_{L_{x}^{2}}d\tau

\displaystyle+\int^{t}_{0}\left\|\nabla_{x}e^{(t-\tau)\triangle}\mathbb{P}\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast\biggl\{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\epsilon,\delta})f^{\varepsilon,\delta}dv\biggr\}\right\|_{L_{x}^{2}}d\tau

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|\nabla u_{in}^{\delta}\|_{L_{x}^{2}}+\sum_{i=1}^{3}A_{2i}.

For
A_{21}
, by using Hölder’s inequality and Lemma
A.2
again, we have

\displaystyle A_{21}

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{0}^{t}(t-\tau)^{-\frac{1}{2}}d\tau\|(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta})\cdot\nabla u^{\varepsilon,\delta}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}))}

\displaystyle\leq C(\varepsilon)T^{\frac{1}{2}}\|u^{\varepsilon,\delta}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}))}\|\nabla u^{\varepsilon,\delta}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}))}.

For
A_{22}
, by Lemma
2.2
it follows that

\displaystyle A_{22}

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{0}^{t}(t-\tau)^{-\frac{19}{20}}d\tau\big\|m_{1}|f^{\varepsilon,\delta}|\big\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{\frac{5}{4}}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}))}

\displaystyle\leq CT^{\frac{1}{20}}\left(\frac{4}{3}\pi\|f^{\varepsilon,\delta}(t,x,v)\|_{L^{\infty}_{txv}}+1\right)\left\||v|^{2}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{6}_{xv}))}^{\frac{4}{5}}.

For
A_{23}
, we derive

\displaystyle A_{23}

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{0}^{t}(t-\tau)^{-\frac{13}{20}}d\tau\left\|\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|f^{\varepsilon,\delta}|dv\right\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{\frac{5}{3}}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}))}

\displaystyle\leq CT^{\frac{7}{20}}\left(\frac{4}{3}\pi\|f^{\varepsilon,\delta}(t,x,v)\|_{L^{\infty}_{txv}}+1\right)\left\|\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|v|^{2}|f^{\varepsilon,\delta}|dv\right)^{\frac{3}{5}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{\frac{5}{3}}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}))}

\displaystyle\leq CT^{\frac{7}{20}}\left(\frac{4}{3}\pi\|f^{\varepsilon,\delta}(t,x,v)\|_{L^{\infty}_{txv}}+1\right)\|\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta}\|_{L^{\infty}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}))}\left\||v|^{2}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{6}_{xv}))}^{\frac{3}{5}}

\displaystyle\leq C(\varepsilon)T^{\frac{7}{20}}\left(\frac{4}{3}\pi\|f^{\varepsilon,\delta}(t,x,v)\|_{L^{\infty}_{txv}}+1\right)\|u^{\varepsilon,\delta}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}))}\left\||v|^{2}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{6}_{xv}))}^{\frac{3}{5}}.

By (
1.11
), we obtain

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{x}\Phi_{1}(t,\cdot)\|_{L_{x}^{2}}\leq

\displaystyle\|\nabla u_{in}^{\delta}\|_{L_{x}^{2}}+C(\varepsilon,R)T^{\frac{1}{20}}\quad\text{for all}~t\in(0,T).

Estimation of
\Phi_{2}
.
By integration by parts for
\Phi_{2}
and Lemma
2.1
we have

\displaystyle\Phi_{2}(t,\cdot)=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{6}}G(t,x,v;0,\xi,\rho)f_{in}^{\delta}(\xi,\rho)d\xi d\rho+\int^{t}_{0}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{6}}\nabla_{\rho}G(t,x,v;\tau,\xi,\rho)

\displaystyle\cdot\left[\left(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta}-\rho\gamma_{\delta}(\rho)\right)f^{\varepsilon,\delta}(\xi,\rho)\right]d\xi d\rho d\tau

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{6}}G(t,x,v;0,\xi,\rho)f_{in}^{\delta}(\xi,\rho)d\xi d\rho+C\int^{t}_{0}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{6}}(t-\tau)^{-\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle G(t,x/2,v/2;\tau,\xi/2,\rho/2)\left|\left(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta}-\rho\gamma_{\delta}(\rho)\right)f^{\varepsilon,\delta}(\xi,\rho)\right|d\xi d\rho d\tau,

which implies that

\displaystyle\|\Phi_{2}(t,\cdot)\|_{L^{1}_{xv}}\leq

\displaystyle\|f_{in}^{\delta}\|_{L^{1}_{xv}}+C\int^{t}_{0}(t-\tau)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{6}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{6}}\quad G(t,x/2,v/2;\tau,\xi/2,\rho/2)

\displaystyle\left|\left(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta}-\rho\gamma_{\delta}(\rho)\right)f^{\varepsilon,\delta}(\xi,\rho)\right|d\xi d\rho dxdvd\tau

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|f_{in}^{\delta}\|_{L^{1}_{xv}}+C\int^{t}_{0}(t-\tau)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta}-v\gamma_{\delta}(v))f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\|_{L^{1}_{xv}}d\tau

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|f_{in}^{\delta}\|_{L^{1}_{xv}}+C(\varepsilon)T^{\frac{1}{2}}(\|f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{6}_{xv}))}+\|f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{6}_{xv}))}^{2}

\displaystyle+\left\||v|^{2}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{6}_{xv}))}+\|u^{\varepsilon,\delta}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}))}^{2})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|f_{in}^{\delta}\|_{L^{1}_{xv}}+C(\varepsilon,R)T^{\frac{1}{2}}\quad\text{for all}~t\in(0,T),

and

\displaystyle\|\Phi_{2}(t,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}_{xv}}\leq

\displaystyle\|f_{in}^{\delta}\|_{L^{\infty}_{xv}}+C\int^{t}_{0}(t-\tau)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\left\||\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta}-v\gamma_{\delta}(v)|f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{xv}}d\tau

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|f_{in}\|_{L^{\infty}_{xv}}+CT^{\frac{1}{2}}(\varepsilon)\|u^{\varepsilon,\delta}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}))}\|f^{\varepsilon,\delta}(t,x,v)\|_{L^{\infty}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3})}

\displaystyle+C(\delta)T^{\frac{1}{2}}\|f^{\varepsilon,\delta}(t,x,v)\|_{L^{\infty}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|f_{in}^{\delta}\|_{L^{\infty}_{xv}}+C(\varepsilon,\delta,R)T^{\frac{1}{2}}\quad\text{for all}~t\in(0,T).

Estimation of
\Phi_{3}
.
Note that

\displaystyle\|\Phi_{3}(t,\cdot)\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{6}_{xv})}\leq

\displaystyle\left\|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{6}}G(t,x,v;0,\xi,\rho)h_{in}^{\delta}(\xi,\rho)d\xi d\rho\right\|_{L^{1}_{xv}}

\displaystyle+\left\|\int^{t}_{0}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{6}}G(t,x,v;\tau,\xi,\rho)\nabla_{\rho}\cdot\left[\left(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta}-\rho\gamma_{\delta}(\rho)\right)h^{\varepsilon,\delta}(\xi,\rho)\right]d\xi d\rho d\tau\right\|_{L^{1}_{xv}}

\displaystyle+2\left\|\int^{t}_{0}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{6}}G(t,x,v;\tau,\xi,\rho)\rho\cdot\left[\left(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta}-\rho\gamma_{\delta}(\rho)\right)f^{\varepsilon,\delta}(\xi,\rho)\right]d\xi d\rho d\tau\right\|_{L^{1}_{xv}}

\displaystyle+6\left\|\int^{t}_{0}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{6}}G(t,x,v;\tau,\xi,\rho)f^{\epsilon,\delta}d\xi d\rho d\tau\right\|_{L^{1}_{xv}}

\displaystyle+4\left\|\int^{t}_{0}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{6}}G(t,x,v;\tau,\xi,\rho)\rho\cdot\nabla_{\rho}f^{\epsilon,\delta}d\xi d\rho d\tau\right\|_{L^{1}_{xv}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum^{5}_{i=1}{A_{3i}}.

For
A_{31}
, by Lemma
2.1
we have

\displaystyle\left\|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{6}}G(t,x,v;0,\xi,\rho)h_{in}^{\delta}(\xi,\rho)d\xi d\rho\right\|_{L^{1}_{xv}}\leq\|h_{in}^{\delta}\|_{L^{1}_{xv}}.

For
A_{32}
, by integration by parts and Hölder’s inequality, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\int^{t}_{0}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{6}}G(t,x,v;\tau,\xi,\rho)\nabla_{\rho}\cdot\left[\left(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta}-\rho\gamma_{\delta}(\rho)\right)h^{\varepsilon,\delta}(\xi,\rho)\right]d\xi d\rho d\tau\right\|_{L^{1}_{xv}}

\displaystyle=\left\|\int^{t}_{0}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{6}}\nabla_{\rho}G(t,x,v;\tau,\xi,\rho)\cdot\left[\left(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta}-\rho\gamma_{\delta}(\rho)\right)h^{\varepsilon,\delta}(\xi,\rho)\right]d\xi d\rho d\tau\right\|_{L^{1}_{xv}}

\displaystyle\leq C\bigg\|\int^{t}_{0}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{6}}(t-\tau)^{-\frac{1}{2}}G(t,x/2,v/2;\tau,\xi/2,\rho/2)

\displaystyle\hskip 142.26378pt\left|\left(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta}-\rho\gamma_{\delta}(\rho)\right)h^{\varepsilon,\delta}(\xi,\rho)\right|d\xi d\rho d\tau\bigg\|_{L^{1}_{xv}}

\displaystyle\leq CT^{\frac{1}{2}}\left\|\left(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta}-v\gamma_{\delta}(v)\right)h^{\varepsilon,\delta}(x,v)\right\|_{L^{\infty}{(0,T;L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{6}_{xv})})}

\displaystyle\leq CT^{\frac{1}{2}}\left[C(\varepsilon)\|u^{\varepsilon,\delta}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}))}\|h^{\varepsilon,\delta}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{6}_{xv}))}+C(\delta)\|h^{\varepsilon,\delta}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{6}_{xv}))}\right].

For
A_{33}
, by Lemma
2.1
and Lemma
2.2
, we have

\displaystyle 2

\displaystyle\left\|\int^{t}_{0}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{6}}G(t,x,v;\tau,\xi,\rho)\rho\cdot\left[\left(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta}-\rho\gamma_{\delta}(\rho)\right)f^{\varepsilon,\delta}(\xi,\rho)\right]d\xi d\rho d\tau\right\|_{L^{1}_{xv}}

\displaystyle\leq CT\left\|\left(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta}-v\gamma_{\delta}(v)\right)|v|f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{6}_{xv}))}

\displaystyle\leq CT\left(\left\|(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta})|v|f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{6}_{xv}))}+\|h^{\varepsilon,\delta}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{6}_{xv}))}\right)

\displaystyle\leq CT\|\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta}\|_{L^{\infty}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x})}\left\||v|f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{6}_{xv}))}+CT\|h^{\varepsilon,\delta}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{6}_{xv}))}

\displaystyle\leq C(\varepsilon)T\|u^{\varepsilon,\delta}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}))}\left(\|h^{\varepsilon,\delta}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{6}_{xv}))}+\|f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{6}_{xv}))}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 199.16928pt+CT\|h^{\varepsilon,\delta}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{6}_{xv}))}.

For
A_{34}
, we get

\displaystyle 6

\displaystyle\left\|\int^{t}_{0}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{6}}G(t,x,v;\tau,\xi,\rho)f^{\epsilon,\delta}d\xi d\rho d\tau\right\|_{L^{1}_{xv}}

\displaystyle\leq CT\left\|f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{6}_{xv}))}.

For
A_{35}
, by the same methods as in
A_{31}
to
A_{34}
we derive

\displaystyle 4

\displaystyle\left\|\int^{t}_{0}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{6}}G(t,x,v;\tau,\xi,\rho)\rho\cdot\nabla_{\rho}f^{\epsilon,\delta}d\xi d\rho d\tau\right\|_{L^{1}_{xv}}

\displaystyle=4\left\|\int^{t}_{0}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{6}}\nabla_{\rho}G(t,x,v;\tau,\xi,\rho)\cdot\rho f^{\epsilon,\delta}d\xi d\rho d\tau\right\|_{L^{1}_{xv}}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+12\left\|\int^{t}_{0}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{6}}G(t,x,v;\tau,\xi,\rho)f^{\epsilon,\delta}d\xi d\rho d\tau\right\|_{L^{1}_{xv}}

\displaystyle\leq CT^{\frac{1}{2}}\left\||v|f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{6}_{xv}))}+CT\left\|f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{6}_{xv}))}

\displaystyle\leq CT^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\|h^{\varepsilon,\delta}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{6}_{xv}))}+\|f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{6}_{xv}))}\right).

Consequently, we have

\displaystyle\|\Phi_{3}(t,\cdot)\|_{L^{1}_{xv}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3})}\leq

\displaystyle\|h_{in}^{\delta}\|_{L^{1}_{xv}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3})}+C(\varepsilon,\delta,R)T^{\frac{1}{2}}\quad\text{for all}~t\in(0,T).

Combining the estimates of
\Phi_{1}
,
\Phi_{2}
, and
\Phi_{3}
, we get that
\Phi
is a mapping from
\Gamma
to
\Gamma
.

2.1.3.
\Phi
is a contraction mapping on
\Gamma

Using the same way, we obtain the following estimates for (

,

,

) , (

,

.

Estimation of
\Phi_{1}
.
For the
L^{2}
norm of
u^{\varepsilon,\delta}
, similar to the estimates of
A_{11}-A_{13}
, we have

\displaystyle\|\Phi_{1}(u_{1}^{\varepsilon,\delta},f_{1}^{\varepsilon,\delta},h_{1}^{\varepsilon,\delta})-\Phi_{1}(u_{2}^{\varepsilon,\delta},f_{2}^{\varepsilon,\delta},h_{2}^{\varepsilon,\delta})\|_{L_{x}^{2}}=

\displaystyle\bigg\|-\int^{t}_{0}e^{(t-\tau)\triangle}\mathbb{P}\bigg\{(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u_{2}^{\varepsilon,\delta}-\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u_{1}^{\varepsilon,\delta})\cdot\nabla u_{1}^{\varepsilon,\delta}+(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u_{2}^{\varepsilon,\delta})\cdot\nabla_{x}(u_{2}^{\varepsilon,\delta}-u_{1}^{\varepsilon,\delta})\bigg\}d\tau

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}e^{(t-\tau)\triangle}\mathbb{P}\bigg\{\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast\int_{\mathbb{R}_{v}^{3}}v(f_{2}^{\varepsilon,\delta}-f_{1}^{\varepsilon,\delta})dv\bigg\}d\tau

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}e^{(t-\tau)\triangle}\mathbb{P}\bigg\{\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast\int_{\mathbb{R}_{v}^{3}}(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u_{2}^{\varepsilon,\delta})(f_{1}^{\varepsilon,\delta}-f_{2}^{\varepsilon,\delta})dv\bigg\}d\tau

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}e^{(t-\tau)\triangle}\mathbb{P}\bigg\{\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast\int_{\mathbb{R}_{v}^{3}}(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u_{1}^{\varepsilon,\delta}-\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u_{2}^{\varepsilon,\delta})f_{1}^{\varepsilon,\delta}dv\bigg\}d\tau\bigg\|_{L^{2}_{x}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle CT^{\frac{1}{4}}\{\|u_{2}^{\varepsilon,\delta}-u_{1}^{\varepsilon,\delta}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}))}\|\nabla u_{1}^{\varepsilon,\delta}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}))}

\displaystyle\hskip 85.35826pt+\|u_{2}^{\varepsilon,\delta}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}))}\|\nabla u_{2}^{\varepsilon,\delta}-\nabla u_{1}^{\varepsilon,\delta}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}))}\}

\displaystyle+CT^{\frac{11}{20}}\left(\frac{4}{3}\pi\|f_{2}^{\varepsilon,\delta}(t,x,v)-f_{1}^{\varepsilon,\delta}(t,x,v)\|_{L^{\infty}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3})}+1\right)\left\||v|^{2}f_{2}^{\varepsilon,\delta}-|v|^{2}f_{1}^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{6}_{xv}))}^{\frac{4}{5}}

\displaystyle+CT^{\frac{1}{4}}\left(\frac{4}{3}\pi\|{f_{1}^{\varepsilon,\delta}(t,x,v)}-f_{2}^{\varepsilon,\delta}(t,x,v)\|_{L^{\infty}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3})}+1\right)\times

\displaystyle\hskip 85.35826pt\left(\|u_{2}^{\varepsilon,\delta}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}))}+\|\nabla u_{2}^{\varepsilon,\delta}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}))}\right)\left\||v|^{2}f_{1}^{\varepsilon,\delta}-|v|^{2}f_{2}^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{6}_{xv}))}^{\frac{3}{5}}

\displaystyle+CT^{\frac{1}{4}}\left(\frac{4}{3}\pi\|f_{1}^{\varepsilon,\delta}(t,x,v)\|_{L^{\infty}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3})}+1\right)\left\||v|^{2}f_{1}^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{6}_{xv}))}^{\frac{3}{5}}\times

\displaystyle\hskip 85.35826pt\Big(\|(u_{1}^{\varepsilon,\delta}-u_{2}^{\varepsilon,\delta})\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}))}+\|\nabla u_{1}^{\varepsilon,\delta}-\nabla u_{2}^{\varepsilon,\delta}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}))}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C(R)T^{\frac{1}{4}}\|(u_{1}^{\varepsilon,\delta},f_{1}^{\varepsilon,\delta},h_{1}^{\varepsilon,\delta})-(u_{2}^{\varepsilon,\delta},f_{2}^{\varepsilon,\delta},h_{2}^{\varepsilon,\delta})\|_{\Gamma}.

For the

\nabla u^{\varepsilon,\delta}
, similar to

, we have

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{x}\Phi_{1}

\displaystyle(u_{1}^{\varepsilon,\delta},f_{1}^{\varepsilon,\delta},h_{1}^{\varepsilon,\delta})-\nabla_{x}\Phi_{1}(u_{2}^{\varepsilon,\delta},f_{2}^{\varepsilon,\delta},h_{2}^{\varepsilon,\delta})\|_{L_{x}^{2}}=

\displaystyle\bigg\|

\displaystyle-\int^{t}_{0}\nabla_{x}e^{(t-\tau)\triangle}\mathbb{P}\left\{(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u_{2}^{\varepsilon,\delta}-\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u_{1}^{\varepsilon,\delta})\cdot\nabla u_{1}^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right\}

\displaystyle-\int^{t}_{0}\nabla_{x}e^{(t-\tau)\triangle}\mathbb{P}\left\{(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u_{2}^{\varepsilon,\delta})\cdot\nabla_{x}(u_{2}^{\varepsilon,\delta}-u_{1}^{\varepsilon,\delta})\right\}d\tau

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}\nabla_{x}e^{(t-\tau)\triangle}\mathbb{P}\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast\left\{\int_{\mathbb{R}_{v}^{3}}v(f_{2}^{\varepsilon,\delta}-f_{1}^{\varepsilon,\delta})dv+(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u_{2}^{\varepsilon,\delta})\int_{\mathbb{R}_{v}^{3}}(f_{1}^{\varepsilon,\delta}-f_{2}^{\varepsilon,\delta})dv\right\}d\tau

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}\nabla_{x}e^{(t-\tau)\triangle}\mathbb{P}\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast\left\{(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u_{1}^{\varepsilon,\delta}-\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u_{2}^{\varepsilon,\delta})\int_{\mathbb{R}_{v}^{3}}f_{1}^{\varepsilon,\delta}dv\right\}d\tau\bigg\|_{L^{2}_{x}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C(\varepsilon)T^{\frac{1}{2}}\bigg\{\|u_{2}^{\varepsilon,\delta}-u_{1}^{\varepsilon,\delta}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}))}\|\nabla u_{1}^{\varepsilon,\delta}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}))}

\displaystyle\hskip 142.26378pt+\|u_{2}^{\varepsilon,\delta}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}))}\|\nabla u_{2}^{\varepsilon,\delta}-\nabla u_{1}^{\varepsilon,\delta}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}))}\bigg\}

\displaystyle+CT^{\frac{1}{20}}\left(\frac{4}{3}\pi\|f_{2}^{\varepsilon,\delta}(t,x,v)-f_{1}^{\varepsilon,\delta}(t,x,v)\|_{L^{\infty}_{txv}}+1\right)\left\||v|^{2}f_{2}^{\varepsilon,\delta}-|v|^{2}f_{1}^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{6}_{xv}))}^{\frac{4}{5}}

\displaystyle+C(\varepsilon)T^{\frac{7}{20}}\left(\frac{4}{3}\pi\|{f_{1}^{\varepsilon,\delta}(t,x,v)}-f_{2}^{\varepsilon,\delta}(t,x,v)\|_{L^{\infty}_{txv}}+1\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 85.35826pt\times\|u_{2}^{\varepsilon,\delta}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2}_{x})}\left\||v|^{2}f_{1}^{\varepsilon,\delta}-|v|^{2}f_{2}^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{6}_{xv}))}^{\frac{3}{5}}

\displaystyle+C(\varepsilon)T^{\frac{7}{20}}\left(\frac{4}{3}\pi\|f_{1}^{\varepsilon,\delta}(t,x,v)\|_{L^{\infty}_{txv}}+1\right)\|u_{1}^{\varepsilon,\delta}-u_{2}^{\varepsilon,\delta}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2}_{x})}\left\||v|^{2}f_{1}^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{6}_{xv}))}^{\frac{3}{5}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C(\varepsilon,R)T^{\frac{1}{20}}\|(u_{1}^{\varepsilon,\delta},f_{1}^{\varepsilon,\delta},h_{1}^{\varepsilon,\delta})-(u_{2}^{\varepsilon,\delta},f_{2}^{\varepsilon,\delta},h_{2}^{\varepsilon,\delta})\|_{\Gamma}.

Estimation of
\Phi_{2}
.
For the
L^{1}
norm of

, we have

\displaystyle\|\Phi_{2}

\displaystyle(u_{1}^{\varepsilon,\delta},f_{1}^{\varepsilon,\delta},h_{1}^{\varepsilon,\delta})-\Phi_{2}(u_{1}^{\varepsilon,\delta},f_{2}^{\varepsilon,\delta},h_{2}^{\varepsilon,\delta})\|_{L_{xv}^{1}}=

\displaystyle\bigg\|\int^{t}_{0}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{6}}(t-\tau)^{-\frac{1}{2}}G(t,x/2,v/2;\tau,\xi/2,\rho/2)\cdot

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\left[\left(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u_{1}^{\varepsilon,\delta}-\rho\gamma_{\delta}(\rho)\right)f_{1}^{\varepsilon,\delta}(\xi,\rho)-\left(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u_{2}^{\varepsilon,\delta}-\rho\gamma_{\delta}(\rho)\right)f_{2}^{\varepsilon,\delta}(\xi,\rho)\right]d\xi d\rho d\tau\bigg\|_{L^{1}_{xv}}

\displaystyle+\|u_{2}^{\varepsilon,\delta}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}))}\|f_{1}^{\varepsilon,\delta}-f_{2}^{\varepsilon,\delta}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{1}_{xv}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}))}\bigg)

\displaystyle+CT^{\frac{1}{2}}\bigg(\|f_{2}^{\varepsilon,\delta}-f_{1}^{\varepsilon,\delta}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{1}_{xv}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}))}+\left\||v|^{2}f_{2}^{\varepsilon,\delta}-|v|^{2}f_{1}^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{1}_{xv}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}))}\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C(\varepsilon,R)T^{\frac{1}{2}}\|(u_{1}^{\varepsilon,\delta},f_{1}^{\varepsilon,\delta},h_{1}^{\varepsilon,\delta})-(u_{2}^{\varepsilon,\delta},f_{2}^{\varepsilon,\delta},h_{2}^{\varepsilon,\delta})\|_{\Gamma}.

For the
L^{\infty}
norm of
f^{\varepsilon,\delta}
, we have

\displaystyle\|\Phi_{2}

\displaystyle(u_{1}^{\varepsilon,\delta},f_{1}^{\varepsilon,\delta},h_{1}^{\varepsilon,\delta})-\Phi_{2}(u_{2}^{\varepsilon,\delta},f_{2}^{\varepsilon,\delta},h_{2}^{\varepsilon,\delta})\|_{L_{xv}^{\infty}}=

\displaystyle\bigg\|

\displaystyle\int^{t}_{0}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{6}}(t-\tau)^{-\frac{1}{2}}G(t,x/2,v/2;\tau,\xi/2,\rho/2)\cdot

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\left[\left(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u_{1}^{\varepsilon,\delta}-\rho\gamma_{\delta}(\rho)\right)f_{1}^{\varepsilon,\delta}(\xi,\rho)-\left(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u_{2}^{\varepsilon,\delta}-\rho\gamma_{\delta}(\rho)\right)f_{2}^{\varepsilon,\delta}(\xi,\rho)\right]d\xi d\rho d\tau\bigg\|_{L^{\infty}_{xv}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C(\varepsilon)T^{\frac{1}{2}}\bigg(\|u_{1}^{\varepsilon,\delta}-u_{2}^{\varepsilon,\delta}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}))}\|f_{1}^{\varepsilon,\delta}\|_{L^{\infty}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3})}

\displaystyle+\|u_{2}^{\varepsilon,\delta}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}))}\|f_{1}^{\varepsilon,\delta}-f_{2}^{\varepsilon,\delta}\|_{L^{\infty}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3})}\bigg)+C(\delta)T^{\frac{1}{2}}\|f_{2}^{\varepsilon,\delta}-f_{1}^{\varepsilon,\delta}\|_{L^{\infty}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C(\varepsilon,\delta,R)T^{\frac{1}{2}}\|(u_{1}^{\varepsilon,\delta},f_{1}^{\varepsilon,\delta},h_{1}^{\varepsilon,\delta})-(u_{2}^{\varepsilon,\delta},f_{2}^{\varepsilon,\delta},h_{2}^{\varepsilon,\delta})\|_{\Gamma}.

Estimation of
\Phi_{3}
.
For
L^{1}
norm of
h^{\varepsilon,\delta}
, similar to
A_{31}-A_{35}
, we have

\displaystyle\|\Phi_{3}(u_{1}^{\varepsilon,\delta},f_{1}^{\varepsilon,\delta},h_{1}^{\varepsilon,\delta})-\Phi_{3}(u_{2}^{\varepsilon,\delta},f_{2}^{\varepsilon,\delta},h_{2}^{\varepsilon,\delta})\|_{L_{xv}^{1}}

\displaystyle=\bigg\|-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{6}}G(t,x,v;\tau,\xi,\rho)\nabla_{\rho}\cdot\big[\big(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u_{2}^{\varepsilon,\delta}-\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u_{1}^{\varepsilon,\delta}\big)h_{2}^{\varepsilon,\delta}(\xi,\rho)\big]\,d\xi\,d\rho\,d\tau

\displaystyle\quad-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{6}}G(t,x,v;\tau,\xi,\rho)\nabla_{\rho}\cdot\big[\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u_{1}^{\varepsilon,\delta}\big(h^{\varepsilon,\delta}_{2}-h^{\varepsilon,\delta}_{1}\big)\big]\,d\xi\,d\rho\,d\tau

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{6}}G(t,x,v;\tau,\xi,\rho)\nabla_{\rho}\cdot\big[\rho\gamma_{\delta}(\rho)\big(h^{\varepsilon,\delta}_{1}-h^{\varepsilon,\delta}_{2}\big)\big]\,d\xi\,d\rho\,d\tau

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{6}}G(t,x,v;\tau,\xi,\rho)\bigg\{2\rho\cdot\Big[\big(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u_{1}^{\varepsilon,\delta}-\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u_{2}^{\varepsilon,\delta}\big)f_{1}^{\varepsilon,\delta}-\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u_{2}^{\varepsilon,\delta}\big(f_{2}^{\varepsilon,\delta}-f_{1}^{\varepsilon,\delta}\big)\Big]\bigg\}

\displaystyle\quad-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{6}}G(t,x,v;\tau,\xi,\rho)\bigg\{2\rho\cdot\big[\rho\gamma_{\delta}(\rho)\big(f_{2}^{\varepsilon,\delta}-f_{1}^{\varepsilon,\delta}\big)\big]\bigg\}

\displaystyle\quad-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{6}}G(t,x,v;\tau,\xi,\rho)\bigg\{6\big(f_{2}^{\varepsilon,\delta}-f_{1}^{\varepsilon,\delta}\big)+4\rho\cdot\big(\nabla_{\rho}f_{2}^{\varepsilon,\delta}-\nabla_{\rho}f_{1}^{\varepsilon,\delta}\big)\bigg\}\,d\xi\,d\rho\,d\tau\bigg\|_{L^{1}_{xv}}

\displaystyle\leq CT^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big(C(\varepsilon)\|u_{2}^{\varepsilon,\delta}-u_{1}^{\varepsilon,\delta}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}))}\|h_{2}^{\varepsilon,\delta}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{6}_{xv}))}

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt+C(\varepsilon)\|u_{1}^{\varepsilon,\delta}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}))}\|h_{2}^{\varepsilon,\delta}-h_{1}^{\varepsilon,\delta}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{6}_{xv}))}+C(\delta)\|h_{1}^{\varepsilon,\delta}-h_{2}^{\varepsilon,\delta}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{6}_{xv}))}\Big)

\displaystyle\quad+C(\varepsilon)T\|u_{1}^{\varepsilon,\delta}-u_{2}^{\varepsilon,\delta}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}))}\left(\|h_{2}^{\varepsilon,\delta}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{6}_{xv}))}+\|f_{2}^{\varepsilon,\delta}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{6}_{xv}))}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+C(\varepsilon)T\|u_{2}^{\varepsilon,\delta}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}))}\left(\|h_{2}^{\varepsilon,\delta}-h_{1}^{\varepsilon,\delta}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{6}_{xv}))}+\|f_{2}^{\varepsilon,\delta}-f_{1}^{\varepsilon,\delta}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{6}_{xv}))}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+CT^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\|f_{2}^{\varepsilon,\delta}-f_{1}^{\varepsilon,\delta}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{6}_{xv}))}+\|h_{2}^{\varepsilon,\delta}-h_{1}^{\varepsilon,\delta}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{6}_{xv}))}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C(\varepsilon,\delta,R)T^{\frac{1}{2}}\|(u_{1}^{\varepsilon,\delta},f_{1}^{\varepsilon,\delta},h_{1}^{\varepsilon,\delta})-(u_{2}^{\varepsilon,\delta},f_{2}^{\varepsilon,\delta},h_{2}^{\varepsilon,\delta})\|_{\Gamma}.

Finally, we obtain

\displaystyle\|\Phi(u_{1}^{\varepsilon,\delta},f_{1}^{\varepsilon,\delta},h_{1}^{\varepsilon,\delta})-\Phi(u_{2}^{\varepsilon,\delta},f_{2}^{\varepsilon,\delta},h_{2}^{\varepsilon,\delta})\|_{\Gamma}\leq C(\varepsilon,\delta,R)T^{\frac{1}{20}}\|(u_{1}^{\varepsilon,\delta},f_{1}^{\varepsilon,\delta},h_{1}^{\varepsilon,\delta})-(u_{2}^{\varepsilon,\delta},f_{2}^{\varepsilon,\delta},h_{2}^{\varepsilon,\delta})\|_{\Gamma},

for all
t\in(0,T)
, which implies that
\Phi
acts as a contraction on
\Gamma
by choosing a sufficiently small
T
. Then, one can conclude the existence of
(u^{\varepsilon,\delta},f^{\varepsilon,\delta},h^{\varepsilon,\delta})
as the fixed point of
\Phi
on
\Gamma
, namely
(u^{\varepsilon,\delta},f^{\varepsilon,\delta},h^{\varepsilon,\delta})=\Phi(u^{\varepsilon,\delta},f^{\varepsilon,\delta},h^{\varepsilon,\delta})
.

2.1.4.
The positivity of
f^{\varepsilon,\delta}

Firstly, set
f^{\varepsilon,\delta}_{+}=\max(0,f^{\varepsilon,\delta})
and
-f^{\varepsilon,\delta}_{-}=\min(0,f^{\varepsilon,\delta})
. Then,
f^{\varepsilon,\delta}=f^{\varepsilon,\delta}_{+}-f^{\varepsilon,\delta}_{-}
. Multiplying the second equation of (
1
) by
f^{\varepsilon,\delta}_{-}
, we derive

\displaystyle\displaystyle\partial_{t}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}_{-}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}_{-}+(v\cdot\nabla_{x})f^{\varepsilon,\delta}_{-}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}_{-}+\nabla_{v}\cdot\left[\left(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta}-v\gamma_{\delta}(v)\right)f^{\varepsilon,\delta}_{-}-\nabla_{v}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}_{-}\right]f^{\varepsilon,\delta}_{-}=0.

Then, by integrating by parts on the above equation, we obtain

\displaystyle\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\partial_{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{6}}(f^{\varepsilon,\delta}_{-})^{2}dxdv+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{6}}|\nabla_{v}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}_{-}|^{2}dxdv+\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{6}}\nabla_{v}(f^{\varepsilon,\delta}_{-})^{2}\cdot(v\gamma_{\delta}(v)-\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta})dxdv=0.

From this equation and
v\cdot\nabla_{v}\gamma_{\delta}(v)\leq 0
, we know that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{6}}\nabla_{v}(f^{\varepsilon,\delta}_{-})^{2}\cdot(v\gamma_{\delta}(v)-\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta})dxdv

\displaystyle=-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{6}}(f^{\varepsilon,\delta}_{-})^{2}\nabla_{v}\cdot(v\gamma_{\delta}(v))dxdv

\displaystyle\geq-3\int_{\mathbb{R}^{6}}(f^{\varepsilon,\delta}_{-})^{2}dxdv.

Hence we get

\displaystyle\displaystyle\partial_{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{6}}(f^{\varepsilon,\delta}_{-})^{2}dxdv+2\int_{\mathbb{R}^{6}}|\nabla_{v}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}_{-}|^{2}dxdv\leq 6\int_{\mathbb{R}^{6}}(f^{\varepsilon,\delta}_{-})^{2}dxdv.

Using Grönwall’s inequality (Lemma
A.4
) and (
1.12
), we can derive

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{6}}(f^{\varepsilon,\delta}_{-})^{2}dxdv\leq e^{6T}\|f_{-}^{\delta}(\cdot,0)\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{xv}}\leq e^{6T}\|f_{-}(\cdot,0)\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{xv}}.

(2.20)

Note that
f_{-}(\cdot,0)=0
due to
f_{in}\geqslant 0
, which means
f^{\varepsilon,\delta}_{-}=0
. Thus
f^{\varepsilon,\delta}=f^{\varepsilon,\delta}_{+}
, and we have
f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\geqslant 0
.

2.2.
Global existence of strong solutions

In order to prove the global existence of strong solutions of this regularized system (
1
), it is enough to prove

\displaystyle\|f^{\varepsilon,\delta}(t,x,v)\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{1}_{xv}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}))}\leq\|f_{in}(t,x,v)\|_{L^{1}_{xv}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3})},

\displaystyle\|f^{\varepsilon,\delta}(t,x,v)\|_{L^{\infty}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3})}\leq e^{3T}\|f_{in}(t,x,v)\|_{L^{\infty}_{xv}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3})},

and

\displaystyle\|u^{\varepsilon,\delta}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}(0,T;{L^{2}_{x}})}+\left\||v|^{2}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;{L^{1}_{xv}})}

(2.21)

\displaystyle+\|\nabla u^{\varepsilon,\delta}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(0,T;{L^{2}_{x}})}+\|\nabla u^{\varepsilon,\delta}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}(0,T;{L^{2}_{x}})}\leq C\left(T+1\right)^{5}e^{C(\varepsilon)(T+1)^{2}e^{2T}}

for any
T>0
, where
C=C\left(\varepsilon,\delta,\|f_{in}\|_{L^{\infty}_{xv}},\|f_{in}\|_{L^{1}_{xv}},\left\||v|^{2}f_{in}\right\|_{L^{1}_{xv}},\|u_{in}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}}\right)
.

2.2.1.
The estimate of
f^{\varepsilon,\delta}

Claim that

\displaystyle\|f^{\varepsilon,\delta}(t,x,v)\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{p}_{xv}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}))}\leq e^{\frac{3(p-1)}{p}T}\|f_{in}(t,x,v)\|_{L^{p}_{xv}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3})}

(2.22)

for all
p\in[1,+\infty]
.

Proof of (
2.22
).

Multiplying
(f^{\varepsilon,\delta})^{p-1}
on both sides of
\eqref{eq:NSVFK0}_{2}
and integrating, we derive

\displaystyle\frac{1}{p}\partial_{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{6}}(f^{\varepsilon,\delta})^{p}dxdv+\frac{1}{p}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{6}}v\cdot\nabla_{x}(f^{\varepsilon,\delta})^{p}dxdv-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{6}}(f^{\varepsilon,\delta})^{p-1}\Delta f^{\varepsilon,\delta}dxdv

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{6}}(f^{\varepsilon,\delta})^{p-1}\nabla_{v}\cdot\left[(v\gamma_{\delta}(v)-\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta})f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right]dxdv,

where
p\in[1,+\infty)
. By integration by parts and
v\cdot\nabla_{v}\gamma\leq 0
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{6}}(f^{\varepsilon,\delta})^{p-1}\nabla_{v}\cdot\left[(v\gamma_{\delta}(v)-\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta})f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right]dxdv

\displaystyle=-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{6}}\nabla_{v}\left[(f^{\varepsilon,\delta})^{p-1}\right]\cdot\left[(v\gamma_{\delta}(v)-\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta})f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right]dxdv

\displaystyle=\frac{p-1}{p}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{6}}(f^{\varepsilon,\delta})^{p}\nabla_{v}\cdot(v\gamma_{\delta}(v))dxdv

\displaystyle\leq\frac{3(p-1)}{p}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{6}}(f^{\varepsilon,\delta})^{p}dxdv.

Then we derive

\displaystyle\partial_{t}

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{6}}(f^{\varepsilon,\delta})^{p}dxdv+\frac{4(p-1)}{p}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{6}}|\nabla_{v}(f^{\varepsilon,\delta})^{\frac{p}{2}}|^{2}dxdv\leq 3(p-1)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{6}}(f^{\varepsilon,\delta})^{p}dxdv.

By Grönwall’s inequality (Lemma
A.4
) and (
1.12
), we get

\displaystyle\|f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{p}_{xv}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{6})}\leq e^{\frac{3(p-1)}{p}T}\|f_{in}^{\delta}\|_{L^{p}_{xv}(\mathbb{R}^{6})}\leq e^{\frac{3(p-1)}{p}T}\|f_{in}\|_{L^{p}_{xv}(\mathbb{R}^{6})}

(2.23)

for arbitrary
p\in[1,+\infty)
.
Thus we get

\displaystyle\|f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\|_{L^{\infty}_{xv}(\mathbb{R}^{6})}\leq e^{3T}\|f_{in}\|_{L^{\infty}_{xv}(\mathbb{R}^{6})}.

MM

\square

2.2.2.
The estimate of
u^{\varepsilon,\delta}
and
|v|^{2}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}

Firstly, we multiply
u^{\varepsilon,\delta}
on the first equation of (
1
) and integrate to get

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\partial_{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(u^{\varepsilon,\delta})^{2}dx+\|\nabla u^{\varepsilon,\delta}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{6}}(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta})\cdot(v-\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta})f^{\varepsilon,\delta}dvdx.

(2.24)

Then, by multiplying
\frac{|v|^{2}}{2}
on the second equation of (
1
) and integrating, we get

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\partial_{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{6}}|v|^{2}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}dxdv-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{6}}

\displaystyle v\cdot(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta}-v)f^{\varepsilon,\delta}dxdv

(2.25)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{6}}\left(1-\gamma_{\delta}(v)\right)|v|^{2}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}dxdv+3\int_{\mathbb{R}^{6}}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}dxdv.

Finally, combining (
2.24
) and (
2.25
), we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\partial_{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}

\displaystyle(u^{\varepsilon,\delta})^{2}dx+\frac{1}{2}\partial_{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{6}}|v|^{2}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}dxdv+\|\nabla u^{\varepsilon,\delta}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{6}}|\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta}-v|^{2}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}dxdv\leq 3\int_{\mathbb{R}^{6}}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}dxdv+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{6}}|v|^{2}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}dxdv.

By Grönwall’s inequality (Lemma
A.4
), (
1.11
) and (
1.12
), we get

\displaystyle\|u^{\varepsilon,\delta}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{3}))}

\displaystyle+\left\||v|^{2}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{1}_{xv}(\mathbb{R}^{6}))}+\|\nabla u^{\varepsilon,\delta}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(0,T;{L^{2}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{3})})}

(2.26)

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\|f_{in}\|_{L^{1}_{xv}(\mathbb{R}^{6})},\left\||v|^{2}f_{in}\right\|_{L^{1}_{xv}(\mathbb{R}^{6})},\|u_{in}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\right)(T+1)e^{2T}.

2.2.3.
The estimate of
\nabla u^{\varepsilon,\delta}

For the first equation of (
1
), taking
\partial_{i}
on both sides of the equation, we get

\displaystyle\partial_{i}\partial_{t}u^{\varepsilon,\delta}+\partial_{i}\left[(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta})\cdot\nabla u^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right]

\displaystyle-\partial_{i}\Delta u^{\varepsilon,\delta}+\partial_{i}\nabla_{x}P^{\varepsilon,\delta}

\displaystyle-\partial_{i}\left\{\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(v-\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta})f^{\varepsilon,\delta}dv\right)\right\}=0.

Then multiplying by
\partial_{i}u^{\varepsilon,\delta}
and taking the integration yields

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\partial_{t}\|

\displaystyle\nabla u^{\varepsilon,\delta}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\|\Delta u^{\varepsilon,\delta}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\partial_{i}u^{\varepsilon,\delta}\partial_{i}\left\{\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(v-\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta})f^{\varepsilon,\delta}dv\right)\right\}dx-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\partial_{i}u^{\varepsilon,\delta}\partial_{i}\left[(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta})\cdot\nabla u^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right]dx

\displaystyle=D_{11}+D_{12}.

For
D_{11}
, by Young’s inequality we have

\displaystyle D_{11}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\partial_{i}^{2}u^{\varepsilon,\delta}\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta}-v)f^{\varepsilon,\delta}dv\right)dx

\displaystyle\leq C(\varepsilon)\left(\|\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+1\right)\|\Delta u^{\varepsilon,\delta}\|_{L^{2}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\left(\left\|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}vf^{\varepsilon,\delta}dv\right\|_{L^{1}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\left\|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}dv\right\|_{L^{1}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\|\Delta u^{\varepsilon,\delta}\|_{L^{2}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}^{2}+C(\varepsilon)\left(\|\ u^{\varepsilon,\delta}\|_{L^{2}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+1\right)^{2}\left(\left\|vf^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right\|_{L^{1}_{xv}(\mathbb{R}^{6})}+\left\|f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right\|_{L^{1}_{xv}(\mathbb{R}^{6})}\right)^{2}.

For
D_{12}
, we have

\displaystyle D_{12}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Delta u^{\varepsilon,\delta}(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta})\cdot\nabla u^{\varepsilon,\delta}dx

\displaystyle\leq\|\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\nabla u^{\varepsilon,\delta}\|_{L^{2}}\|\Delta u^{\varepsilon,\delta}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{16}\|\Delta u^{\varepsilon,\delta}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+C(\varepsilon)\|u^{\varepsilon,\delta}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\|\nabla u^{\varepsilon,\delta}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

Collecting
D_{11}
and
D_{12}
, we have

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\|\nabla u^{\varepsilon,\delta}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\leq

\displaystyle C(\varepsilon)(\|\ u^{\varepsilon,\delta}\|_{L^{2}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+1)^{2}\left(\left\|vf^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right\|_{L^{1}_{xv}(\mathbb{R}^{6})}+\left\|f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right\|_{L^{1}_{xv}(\mathbb{R}^{6})}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle+C(\varepsilon)\|u^{\varepsilon,\delta}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\|\nabla u^{\varepsilon,\delta}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

Using Grönwall’s inequality, (
2.22
) and (
2.26
), we have

\displaystyle\|\nabla u^{\varepsilon,\delta}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}(0,T;{L^{2}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{3})})}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\varepsilon,\delta,\|f_{in}\|_{L^{\infty}_{xv}},\|f_{in}\|_{L^{1}_{xv}}\left\||v|^{2}f_{in}^{\delta}\right\|_{L^{1}_{xv}},\|u_{in}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}}\right)(T+1)^{5}e^{C(\varepsilon)(T+1)^{2}e^{2T}}.

To sum up the above estimates, for any
t\in(0,T)
, we achieve (
2.21
).

Next, we complete the proof of Theorem
1.4
.

Proof.

Using the proof of
\Phi
as a contraction mapping (2.1.2
\&
2.1.3) and the bounded estimates of solutions in (
2.21
), Theorem
1.4
is proved.

MM

\square

3.
Uniform estimates for the regularized system

In this section, we prove some uniform estimates of solutions in Theorem
1.4
.

3.1.
Uniform estimates of
u^{\varepsilon,\delta}
and
f^{\varepsilon,\delta}

It follows from (
2.22
) and (
2.26
) that the following lemma holds:

Lemma 3.1
.

Under the assumptions stated in Theorem
1.4
, let
(u^{\varepsilon,\delta},f^{\varepsilon,\delta},|v|^{2}f^{\varepsilon,\delta})
denote a solution to the system (
1
). Then there exists a positive constant
C
, independent of the parameters
\varepsilon
and
\delta
, such that the following uniform estimate holds:

\displaystyle\|f^{\varepsilon,\delta}(t,x,v)\|

\displaystyle{}_{L^{\infty}(0,t;L^{1}_{xv}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}))}+\|f^{\varepsilon,\delta}(t,x,v)\|_{L^{\infty}((0,t)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\|u^{\varepsilon,\delta}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}(0,t;{L^{2}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{3})})}

(3.27)

\displaystyle+\left\||v|^{2}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(0,t;{L^{1}_{xv}(\mathbb{R}^{6})})}+\|\nabla u^{\varepsilon,\delta}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(0,t;{L^{2}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{3})})}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\|f_{in}\|_{L^{\infty}_{xv}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3})},\|f_{in}\|_{L^{1}_{xv}(\mathbb{R}^{6})},\left\||v|^{2}f_{in}\right\|_{L^{1}_{xv}(\mathbb{R}^{6})},\|u_{in}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\right)(t+1)e^{3t}.

Lemma 3.2
.

Under the assumptions stated in Theorem
1.4
, there exists a constant
C>0
such that

\displaystyle\|f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}(0,t;L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{6}_{xv}))}+2\|\nabla_{v}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\|^{2}_{L^{2}((0,t)\times\mathbb{R}^{6}_{xv})}\leq C\|f_{in}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{6}_{xv})}(1+t)e^{3t}.

(3.28)

Proof.

Multiplying
f^{\varepsilon,\delta}
on the second equation of (
1
) and integrating, we get

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\partial_{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{6}}(f^{\varepsilon,\delta})^{2}dxdv

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{6}}|\nabla_{v}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}|^{2}dxdv=\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{6}}\left(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta}-v\gamma_{\delta}(v)\right)\cdot\nabla_{v}(f^{\varepsilon,\delta})^{2}dxdv.

For the right-hand side, using integration by parts and the fact that
v\cdot\nabla_{v}\gamma_{\delta}\leq 0
, we deduce

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{6}}

\displaystyle\left(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta}-v\gamma_{\delta}(v)\right)\cdot\nabla_{v}(f^{\varepsilon,\delta})^{2}dxdv

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{6}}(f^{\varepsilon,\delta})^{2}\nabla_{v}\cdot\left(v\gamma_{\delta}(v)\right)dxdv

\displaystyle\leq\frac{3}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{6}}(f^{\varepsilon,\delta})^{2}dxdv,

which implies

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{6}}(f^{\varepsilon,\delta})^{2}dxdv+2\int_{\mathbb{R}^{6}}|\nabla_{v}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}|^{2}dxdv\leq 3\int_{\mathbb{R}^{6}}(f^{\varepsilon,\delta})^{2}dxdv.

Applying Grönwall’s inequality and (
1.12
), we get

\displaystyle\|f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}(0,t;L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{6}_{xv}))}+2\|\nabla_{v}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(0,t;L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{6}_{xv}))}\leq C\|f_{in}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{6}_{xv})}^{2}(1+t)e^{3t}.

MM

\square

Lemma 3.3
.

Under the initial conditions prescribed in Theorem
1.4
, there exists a constant
C>0
,
whose value depends solely on the norms of the initial data,
such that the following uniform estimate holds:

\displaystyle\left\||v|^{3}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(0,t;L^{1}_{xv}(\mathbb{R}^{6}))}\leq C(t+1)^{2}\exp\left[C(t+1)^{3}e^{4t}\right],

(3.29)

where

C=C\left(\|f_{\mathrm{in}}\|_{L^{1}_{xv}(\mathbb{R}^{6})},\|f_{\mathrm{in}}\|_{L^{\infty}_{xv}(\mathbb{R}^{6})},\left\||v|^{3}f_{\mathrm{in}}\right\|_{L^{1}_{xv}(\mathbb{R}^{6})},\|u_{\mathrm{in}}\|_{L^{2}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\right).

Proof.

Multiplying the second equation in (
1
) by
|v|^{3}
and integrating over
\mathbb{R}^{6}_{xv}
, by Young’s inequality and (
2.19
) we obtain

\displaystyle\partial_{t}

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}_{xv}^{6}}|v|^{3}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}dxdv+3\int_{\mathbb{R}_{xv}^{6}}|v|^{3}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\gamma_{\delta}(v)dxdv

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}_{xv}^{6}}3|v|v\cdot(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta})f^{\varepsilon,\delta}dxdv+12\int_{\mathbb{R}_{xv}^{6}}|v|f^{\varepsilon,\delta}dxdv

\displaystyle\leq C\|\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta}\|_{L^{6}_{x}{(\mathbb{R}^{3})}}\left\|\int_{\mathbb{R}_{v}^{3}}|v|^{2}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}dv\right\|_{L^{\frac{6}{5}}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+C\int_{\mathbb{R}_{xv}^{6}}|v|^{2}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}dxdv+C\int_{\mathbb{R}_{xv}^{6}}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}dxdv

\displaystyle\leq C\|u^{\varepsilon,\delta}\|_{L^{6}_{x}{(\mathbb{R}^{3}})}\left\||v|^{3}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right\|^{\frac{5}{6}}_{L^{1}_{xv}{(\mathbb{R}^{6})}}\|f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\|^{\frac{1}{6}}_{L^{\infty}_{xv}{(\mathbb{R}^{6})}}

\displaystyle\hskip 142.26378pt+C\int_{\mathbb{R}_{xv}^{6}}|v|^{2}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}dxdv+C\int_{\mathbb{R}_{xv}^{6}}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}dxdv

\displaystyle\leq C\|u^{\varepsilon,\delta}\|^{\frac{6}{5}}_{\dot{H}^{1}_{x}{(\mathbb{R}^{3})}}\left\||v|^{3}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right\|_{L^{1}_{xv}{(\mathbb{R}^{6})}}+C\|f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\|_{L^{\infty}_{xv}{(\mathbb{R}^{6})}}

\displaystyle\hskip 142.26378pt+C\int_{\mathbb{R}_{xv}^{6}}|v|^{2}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}dxdv+C\int_{\mathbb{R}_{xv}^{6}}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}dxdv.

By Grönwall’s inequality, (
1.12
) and (
3.27
), we obtain the following estimate:

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}_{xv}^{6}}|v|^{3}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}dxdv

\displaystyle\leq e^{Ct^{\frac{2}{5}}\|\nabla_{x}u^{\varepsilon,\delta}\|^{\frac{6}{5}}_{L^{2}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x})}}\bigg[Ct\left\||v|^{2}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;{L^{1}_{xv}(\mathbb{R}^{6})})}

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt+\left\||v|^{3}f_{in}\right\|_{L^{1}_{xv}(\mathbb{R}^{6})}+Cte^{3t}\big(\|f_{in}\|_{L^{1}_{xv}(\mathbb{R}^{6})}+\|f_{in}^{\delta}\|_{L^{\infty}_{xv}(\mathbb{R}^{6})}\big)\bigg]

\displaystyle=C(t+1)^{2}e^{C(t+1)^{3}e^{4t}},

where
C=C\left(\|f_{in}\|_{L^{1}_{xv}(\mathbb{R}^{6})},\|f_{in}\|_{L^{\infty}_{xv}(\mathbb{R}^{6})},\left\||v|^{3}f_{in}\right\|_{L^{1}_{xv}(\mathbb{R}^{6})},\|u_{in}\|_{L^{2}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\right)
.

MM

\square

Proposition 3.4
.

Under the assumptions stated in Theorem
1.4
, the following moment estimate holds:

\displaystyle\left\||x|^{2}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right\|

\displaystyle{}_{L^{\infty}(0,t;L^{1}_{xv}(\mathbb{R}^{6}))}

(3.30)

\displaystyle\leq C\Big(\|f_{in}\|_{L^{1}_{xv}},\,\||v|^{2}f_{in}\|_{L^{1}_{xv}},\,\|u_{in}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{2},\,\||x|^{2}f_{in}\|_{L^{1}_{xv}}\Big)(t+1)^{2}\,e^{4t}.

In addition, the following inequality is satisfied:

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}

\displaystyle f^{\varepsilon,\delta}|\log f^{\varepsilon,\delta}|dxdv\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\log f^{\varepsilon,\delta}dxdv

(3.31)

\displaystyle+C\Big(\|f_{in}\|_{L^{1}_{xv}},\,\||v|^{2}f_{in}\|_{L^{1}_{xv}},\,\|u_{in}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{2},\,\||x|^{2}f_{in}\|_{L^{1}_{xv}}\Big)(t+1)^{2}\,e^{4t}.

Proof.

Multiplying the second equation in (
1
) by
|x|^{2}
, and integrating over the spatial and velocity variables, it follows that

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{v}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{x}^{3}}|x|^{2}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}dxdv

\displaystyle=2\int_{\mathbb{R}_{v}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{x}^{3}}\left(v\cdot x\right)f^{\varepsilon,\delta}dxdv
