\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}_{v}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{x}^{3}}|v|^{2}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}dxdv+\int_{\mathbb{R}_{v}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{x}^{3}}|x|^{2}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}dxdv.

By Grönwall’s inequality together with estimates (
3.27
) and (
1.12
), one obtains

\displaystyle\|x^{2}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\|

{}_{L^{\infty}\left(0,t;L^{1}_{xv}\left(\mathbb{R}^{6}\right)\right)}

\displaystyle\leq C(\|f_{in}\|_{L^{1}_{xv}(\mathbb{R}^{6})},\left\||v|^{2}f_{in}\right\|_{L^{1}_{xv}(\mathbb{R}^{6})},\|u_{in}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{3})},\left\||x|^{2}f_{in}\right\|_{L^{1}_{xv}(\mathbb{R}^{6})})(t+1)^{2}e^{4t}.

Next, observe that

\displaystyle f^{\varepsilon,\delta}|\log f^{\varepsilon,\delta}|

\displaystyle=f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\log f^{\varepsilon,\delta}-2f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\log f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\chi_{0\leq f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\leq 1}

\displaystyle=f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\log f^{\varepsilon,\delta}-2f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\log f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\chi_{e^{-\omega}\leq f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\leq 1}-2f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\log f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\chi_{e^{-\omega}\geq f^{\varepsilon,\delta}}

\displaystyle\leq f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\log f^{\varepsilon,\delta}+2f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\omega+C\sqrt{f^{\varepsilon,\delta}}\chi_{e^{-\omega}\geq f^{\varepsilon,\delta}}

\displaystyle\leq f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\log f^{\varepsilon,\delta}+2f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\omega+Ce^{-\frac{\omega}{2}}\quad\left(\omega\geq 0\right).

Setting
\omega=\frac{x^{2}+v^{2}}{8}
, it follows that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}_{v}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{x}^{3}}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}|\log f^{\varepsilon,\delta}|dxdv

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}_{v}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{x}^{3}}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\log f^{\varepsilon,\delta}dxdv+\frac{1}{4}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{v}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{x}^{3}}\left(x^{2}+v^{2}\right)f^{\varepsilon,\delta}dxdv

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt+2C\int_{\mathbb{R}_{v}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{x}^{3}}e^{-\frac{x^{2}+v^{2}}{16}}dxdv.

Finally, the inequality (
3.31
) follows from a combination of (
3.27
) and (
3.30
).

MM

\square

Lemma 3.5
.

Under the assumptions stated in Theorem
1.4
, there holds

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{v}\sqrt{f^{\varepsilon,\delta}}\|_{L^{2}((0,t)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3})}\leq C(t+1)^{3}e^{9t},

(3.32)

where

\displaystyle C=C\left(\|f_{in}\|_{L^{\infty}_{xv}},\|f_{in}\|_{L^{1}_{xv}},\left\||v|^{2}f_{in}\right\|_{L^{1}_{xv}},\|u_{in}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}},\|f_{in}\log f_{in}\|_{L^{1}_{xv}},\left\||x|^{2}f_{in}\right\|_{L^{1}_{xv}}\right).

Proof.

First, multiplying
1+\frac{|v|^{2}}{2}+\log f^{\varepsilon,\delta}
on the second equation of (
1
) and integrating over
\mathbb{R}^{6}
yields:

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{6}_{xv}}\partial_{t}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}

\displaystyle\left(1+\frac{|v|^{2}}{2}+\log f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right)dxdv+\int_{\mathbb{R}_{xv}^{6}}(v\cdot\nabla_{x})f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\left(1+\frac{|v|^{2}}{2}+\log f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right)dxdv

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathbb{R}_{xv}^{6}}\nabla_{v}\cdot\left[\left(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta}-v\gamma_{\delta}(v)\right)f^{\varepsilon,\delta}-\nabla_{v}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right]\left(1+\frac{|v|^{2}}{2}+\log f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right)dxdv

\displaystyle\hskip 85.35826pt:=e_{1}+e_{2}+e_{3}=0,

where

\displaystyle e_{1}

\displaystyle=\partial_{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{6}_{xv}}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\left(1+\frac{|v|^{2}}{2}+\log f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right)dxdv-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{6}_{xv}}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\partial_{t}\left(1+\frac{|v|^{2}}{2}+\log f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right)dxdv

\displaystyle=\partial_{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{6}_{xv}}\left(\frac{|v|^{2}}{2}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}+f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\log f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right)dxdv;

\displaystyle e_{2}

\displaystyle=-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{6}_{xv}}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\frac{1}{f^{\varepsilon,\delta}}\nabla_{x}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\cdot vdxdv

\displaystyle=0;

and

\displaystyle e_{3}=

\displaystyle-\int_{\mathbb{R}_{xv}^{6}}\left[\left(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta}-v\gamma_{\delta}(v)\right)f^{\varepsilon,\delta}-\nabla_{v}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right]\left(v+\frac{\nabla_{v}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}}{f^{\varepsilon,\delta}}\right)dxdv

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}_{xv}^{6}}\frac{|(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta}-v)f^{\varepsilon,\delta}-\nabla_{v}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}|^{2}}{f^{\varepsilon,\delta}}dxdv-\int_{\mathbb{R}_{xv}^{6}}(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta})\cdot(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta}-v)f^{\varepsilon,\delta}dxdv

\displaystyle-\int_{\mathbb{R}_{xv}^{6}}|v|^{2}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}(1-\gamma_{\delta}(v))dxdv-\int_{\mathbb{R}_{xv}^{6}}v\cdot\nabla_{v}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}(1-\gamma_{\delta}(v))dxdv.

To sum up, it becomes

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{6}_{xv}}\left(\frac{|v|^{2}}{2}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}+f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\log f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right)(t,\cdot)dxdv+\int_{\left(0,t\right)\times\mathbb{R}_{v}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{x}^{3}}\frac{|(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta}-v)f^{\varepsilon,\delta}-\nabla_{v}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}|^{2}}{f^{\varepsilon,\delta}}dxdvds

(3.33)

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\left(0,t\right)\times\mathbb{R}_{v}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{x}^{3}}(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta})\cdot(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta}-v)f^{\varepsilon,\delta}dxdvds+\int_{\left(0,t\right)\times\mathbb{R}_{v}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{x}^{3}}v\cdot\nabla_{v}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}(1-\gamma_{\delta}(v))dxdvds

\displaystyle-\int_{\left(0,t\right)\times\mathbb{R}_{v}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{x}^{3}}|v|^{2}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}(\gamma_{\delta}(v)-1)dxdvds+\int_{\mathbb{R}_{v}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{x}^{3}}\left(\frac{|v|^{2}}{2}f_{in}+f_{in}\log f_{in}\right)dxdv

\displaystyle:=S_{1}+S_{2}+S_{3}+S_{4}.

For
S_{1}
, by (
2.17
), it shows that

\displaystyle S_{1}\leq

\displaystyle C\left(\frac{4}{3}\pi e^{3t}\|f_{in}(t,x,v)\|_{L^{\infty}_{txv}}+1\right)\int_{(0,t)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}\left|\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right|^{2}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}|v|^{\frac{3}{2}}dv\right)^{\frac{2}{3}}dxds

\displaystyle+C\left(\frac{4}{3}\pi e^{3t}\|f_{in}(t,x,v)\|_{L^{\infty}_{txv}}+1\right)\left\||v|^{2}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right\|_{L^{\infty}\left(0,t;L^{1}_{xv}(\mathbb{R}^{6})\right)}^{\frac{4}{5}}\left\|u^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right\|_{L^{1}(0,t;L^{5}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x})}.

Due to the embedding inequality

\displaystyle\|u\|_{L_{t}^{q}L_{x}^{p}}^{2}\leq C\left(\|u\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}L_{x}^{2}}^{2}+\|\nabla u\|_{L_{t}^{2}L_{x}^{2}}^{2}\right),\quad\text{for}\quad\frac{2}{q}+\frac{3}{p}=\frac{3}{2},~2\leq p\leq 6,

(3.34)

using (
3.27
) and Hölder’s inequality, we have

\displaystyle S_{1}\leq

\displaystyle C\left(\frac{4}{3}\pi e^{3t}\|f_{in}(t,x,v)\|_{L^{\infty}_{txv}}+1\right)\int_{(0,t)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}\left|\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right|^{2}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}|v|^{\frac{3}{2}}dv\right)^{\frac{2}{3}}dxds

\displaystyle+C(t+1)\left(\frac{4}{3}\pi e^{3t}\|f_{in}(t,x,v)\|_{L^{\infty}_{txv}}+1\right)\left\||v|^{2}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right\|_{L^{\infty}\left(0,t;L^{1}_{xv}(\mathbb{R}^{6})\right)}^{\frac{4}{5}}\times

\displaystyle\left(\|u^{\varepsilon,\delta}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,t;L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}))}+\|\nabla u^{\varepsilon,\delta}\|_{L^{2}(0,t;L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}))}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C(t+1)\left(\frac{4}{3}\pi e^{3t}\|f_{in}(t,x,v)\|_{L^{\infty}_{txv}}+1\right)\left(\|u^{\varepsilon,\delta}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}(0,t;L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}))}+\|\nabla u^{\varepsilon,\delta}\|_{L^{2}(0,t;L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}))}^{2}+1\right)\times

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\left(\|f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\|_{L^{\infty}\left(0,t;L^{1}_{xv}(\mathbb{R}^{6})\right)}^{\frac{2}{3}}+\left\||v|^{2}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right\|_{L^{\infty}\left(0,t;L^{1}_{xv}(\mathbb{R}^{6})\right)}^{\frac{2}{3}}+\left\||v|^{2}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right\|_{L^{\infty}\left(0,t;L^{1}_{xv}(\mathbb{R}^{6})\right)}^{\frac{4}{5}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\left(\|f_{in}\|_{L^{\infty}_{xv}(\mathbb{R}^{6})},\|f_{in}\|_{L^{1}_{xv}(\mathbb{R}^{6})},\left\||v|^{2}f_{in}\right\|_{L^{1}_{xv}(\mathbb{R}^{6})},\|u_{in}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\right)(t+1)^{4}e^{12t}.

Due to
v\cdot\nabla_{v}\gamma\leq 0
,
S_{2}
yields:

\displaystyle S_{2}=

\displaystyle-3\int_{\left(0,t\right)\times\mathbb{R}_{v}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{x}^{3}}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}(1-\gamma_{\delta}(v))dxdvds+\int_{\left(0,t\right)\times\mathbb{R}_{v}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{x}^{3}}v\cdot\nabla_{v}\gamma_{\delta}(v)f^{\varepsilon,\delta}dxdvds\leq 0.

For
S_{3}
, applying (
3.27
) gives:

\displaystyle S_{3}

\displaystyle\leq C(t)\left\||v|^{2}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right\|_{L^{\infty}\left(0,t;L^{1}_{xv}(\mathbb{R}^{6})\right)}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\|f_{in}\|_{L^{1}_{xv}(\mathbb{R}^{6})},\left\||v|^{2}f_{in}\right\|_{L^{1}_{xv}(\mathbb{R}^{6})},\|u_{in}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\right)(t+1)^{2}e^{3t}.

To sum up, we derive

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{6}_{xv}}

\displaystyle\left(\frac{1}{2}|v|^{2}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}+f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\log f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right)(t,\cdot)dxdv+\int_{(0,t)\times\mathbb{R}_{xv}^{6}}\frac{|(u^{\varepsilon,\delta}-v)f^{\varepsilon,\delta}-\nabla_{v}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}|^{2}}{f^{\varepsilon,\delta}}dxdvds

(3.35)

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\|f_{in}\|_{L^{\infty}_{xv}},\|f_{in}\|_{L^{1}_{xv}},\left\||v|^{2}f_{in}\right\|_{L^{1}_{xv}},\|u_{in}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}},\|f_{in}\log f_{in}\|_{L^{1}_{xv}}\right)(t+1)^{4}e^{12t}.

Moreover, the following identity holds:

\displaystyle\int_{(0,t)\times\mathbb{R}_{xv}^{6}}\frac{|(u^{\varepsilon,\delta}-v)f^{\varepsilon,\delta}-\nabla_{v}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}|^{2}}{f^{\varepsilon,\delta}}dxdvds

\displaystyle=\int_{(0,t)\times\mathbb{R}_{xv}^{6}}|u^{\varepsilon,\delta}-v|^{2}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}dxdvds+4\int_{(0,t)\times\mathbb{R}_{xv}^{6}}\left|\nabla_{v}\sqrt{f^{\varepsilon,\delta}}\right|^{2}dxdvds

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt-4\int_{(0,t)\times\mathbb{R}_{xv}^{6}}(u^{\varepsilon,\delta}-v)\cdot\nabla_{v}\sqrt{f^{\varepsilon,\delta}}\sqrt{f^{\varepsilon,\delta}}dxdvds

\displaystyle=\int_{(0,t)\times\mathbb{R}_{xv}^{6}}|u^{\varepsilon,\delta}-v|^{2}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}dxdvds+4\int_{(0,t)\times\mathbb{R}_{xv}^{6}}\left|\nabla_{v}\sqrt{f^{\varepsilon,\delta}}\right|^{2}dxdvds

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt-6\int_{(0,t)\times\mathbb{R}_{xv}^{6}}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}dxdvds.

According to (
3.35
), it yields

\displaystyle 4\int_{(0,t)\times\mathbb{R}_{xv}^{6}}

\displaystyle\left|\nabla_{v}\sqrt{f^{\varepsilon,\delta}}\right|^{2}dxdvds+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{6}_{xv}}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\log f^{\varepsilon,\delta}(t,\cdot)dxdv\leq

\displaystyle C\left(\|f_{in}\|_{L^{\infty}_{xv}},\|f_{in}\|_{L^{1}_{xv}},\left\||v|^{2}f_{in}\right\|_{L^{1}_{xv}},\|u_{in}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}},\|f_{in}\log f_{in}\|_{L^{1}_{xv}}\right)(t+1)^{4}e^{12t}.

According to (
3.31
) in Proposition
3.4
, the estimate (
3.32
) can be established.

MM

\square

3.2.
The estimates of
|v|^{\kappa}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}

We first obtain the estimates on
u^{\varepsilon,\delta}
by Tao’s method.

Lemma 3.6
.

Under the initial hypotheses of Theorem
1.4
,
the following estimate holds for all
p\in[2,+\infty)
:

\displaystyle\|u^{\varepsilon,\delta}\|_{L^{1}(0,t;L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}))}\leq C(t+1)^{4}\exp\left[C(t+1)^{3}e^{4t}\right],

(3.36)

where the constant
C>0
depends explicitly on
p
and the norms of the initial data:

C=C\left(\|f_{\mathrm{in}}\|_{L^{1}_{xv}(\mathbb{R}^{6})},\|f_{\mathrm{in}}\|_{L^{\infty}_{xv}(\mathbb{R}^{6})},\||v|^{3}f_{\mathrm{in}}\|_{L^{1}_{xv}(\mathbb{R}^{6})},\|u_{\mathrm{in}}\|_{L^{2}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\right).

Proof.

Let

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636ptu^{\varepsilon,\delta}

\displaystyle=e^{t\triangle}u_{in}^{\delta}-\int^{t}_{0}e^{(t-\tau)\triangle}\mathbb{P}\bigg\{(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta})\cdot\nabla u^{\varepsilon,\delta}-\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast\left\{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(v-\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta})f^{\varepsilon,\delta}dv\right\}\bigg\}(\tau,\cdot)d\tau

\displaystyle=e^{t\triangle}u_{in}^{\delta}-\int^{t}_{0}e^{(t-\tau)\triangle}\mathbb{P}\left((\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta})\cdot\nabla u^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right)d\tau+\int^{t}_{0}e^{(t-\tau)\triangle}\mathbb{P}\left(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}vf^{\varepsilon,\delta}dv\right)d\tau

\displaystyle\hskip 142.26378pt-\int^{t}_{0}e^{(t-\tau)\triangle}\mathbb{P}\left(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}dv\right)d\tau

\displaystyle=m_{1}+m_{2}+m_{3}+m_{4}.

For
m_{1}
, by Lemma
A.2
and (
1.11
), this yields

\displaystyle\|e^{t\triangle}u_{in}^{\delta}\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{p}_{x}}\leq Ct^{\frac{p+6}{4p}}\|u_{in}\|_{L^{2}_{x}},~p\geq 2.

For
m_{2}
, by Hölder’s inequality and the embedding inequality (
3.34
), this becomes

\displaystyle\|m_{2}\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{\frac{3}{2}}_{x}}

\displaystyle\leq Ct\|(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta})\cdot\nabla u^{\varepsilon,\delta}\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{\frac{3}{2}}_{x}}

(3.37)

\displaystyle\leq Ct\|u^{\varepsilon,\delta}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{6}_{x}}\|\nabla u^{\varepsilon,\delta}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}

\displaystyle\leq Ct\|\nabla u^{\varepsilon,\delta}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}^{2}.

Then, as in Proposition 9.1 of
[
62
]
, we define the Littlewood–Paley projection operators on
\mathbb{R}^{3}
. Let
\varphi(\xi)
be a fixed bump function supported in the ball
\{\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{3}:|\xi|\leq 2\}
and equal to
1
on the ball
\{\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{3}:|\xi|\leq 1\}
. Define a
dyadic number
to be a number
N
of the form
N=2^{k}
for some integer
k
. For each dyadic number
N
, we define the Fourier multipliers

\widehat{P_{N}f}(\xi):=\psi(\xi/N)\hat{f}(\xi):=(\varphi(\xi/N)-\varphi(2\xi/N))\hat{f}(\xi).

Thus for any tempered distribution, we have
f=\sum_{N}P_{N}f
in a weakly convergent sense at least, where the sum ranges over dyadic numbers. By Littlewood-Paley decomposition and Minkowski’s inequality, we have

\displaystyle\left\|m_{2}\right\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}

\displaystyle=\left\|\int_{0}^{t}e^{(t-\tau)\triangle}\mathbb{P}\left(\nabla\cdot(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta}\otimes u^{\varepsilon,\delta})\right)d\tau\right\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{N}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{t}\left\|P_{N}e^{(t-\tau)\triangle}\mathbb{P}\left(\nabla\cdot(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta}\otimes u^{\varepsilon,\delta})\right)\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}d\tau dt.

Using Lemma 2.2 of
[
62
]
and bounding the first-order operator
\mathbb{P}\triangle\sim-N^{2}
on the range of
P_{N}
, we may bound this by

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{N}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{t}\exp\left(-c(t-\tau)N^{2}\right)N\left\|\left(P_{N}(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta}\otimes u^{\varepsilon,\delta})\right)\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}d\tau dt

for some
c>0
; interchanging integrals and evaluating the
\tau
integral, this becomes

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{N}\int_{0}^{T}N^{-1}\left\|\left(P_{N}(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta}\otimes u^{\varepsilon,\delta})\right)\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}dt.

(3.38)

We now apply the Littlewood-Paley trichotomy to write

\displaystyle P_{N}(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta}

\displaystyle\otimes u^{\varepsilon,\delta})=\sum_{N_{1}\sim N}\sum_{N_{2}\lesssim N}P_{N}(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta}_{N_{1}}\otimes u^{\varepsilon,\delta}_{N_{2}})

\displaystyle+\sum_{N_{2}\sim N}\sum_{N_{1}\lesssim N}P_{N}(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta}_{N_{1}}\otimes u^{\varepsilon,\delta}_{N_{2}})+\sum_{N_{1}\gtrsim N}\sum_{N_{2}\sim N_{1}}P_{N}(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta}_{N_{1}}\otimes u^{\varepsilon,\delta}_{N_{2}}),

where
u_{N}:=P_{N}u
. For
N_{1},N_{2}
in the first sum, Bernstein’s inequality (Lemma
A.9
) yields that

\displaystyle\left\|P_{N}(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta}_{N_{1}}\otimes u^{\varepsilon,\delta}_{N_{2}})\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|u^{\varepsilon,\delta}_{N_{1}}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\|u^{\varepsilon,\delta}_{N_{2}}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}

\displaystyle\lesssim N_{1}^{\frac{3}{2}}N_{2}^{\frac{3}{2}}\|u^{\varepsilon,\delta}_{N_{1}}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\|u^{\varepsilon,\delta}_{N_{2}}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}

\displaystyle\lesssim N(N_{2}/N_{1})^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\nabla u^{\varepsilon,\delta}_{N_{1}}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\|\nabla u^{\varepsilon,\delta}_{N_{2}}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}.

For the second sum, as for the proof in the first sum, we have

\displaystyle\left\|P_{N}(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta}_{N_{1}}\otimes u^{\varepsilon,\delta}_{N_{2}})\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}

\displaystyle\lesssim N(N_{1}/N_{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\nabla u^{\varepsilon,\delta}_{N_{1}}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\|\nabla u^{\varepsilon,\delta}_{N_{2}}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}.

For the third sum, we use Bernstein’s inequality (Lemma
A.9
) in a slightly different way to estimate

\displaystyle\left\|P_{N}(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta}_{N_{1}}\otimes u^{\varepsilon,\delta}_{N_{2}})\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}

\displaystyle\lesssim N^{3}\left\|\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta}_{N_{1}}\otimes u^{\varepsilon,\delta}_{N_{2}}\right\|_{L^{1}_{x}}

\displaystyle\lesssim N^{3}\left\|u^{\varepsilon,\delta}_{N_{1}}\right\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\left\|u^{\varepsilon,\delta}_{N_{2}}\right\|_{L^{2}_{x}}

\displaystyle\lesssim N(N/N_{1})^{2}\left\|\nabla u^{\varepsilon,\delta}_{N_{1}}\right\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\left\|\nabla u^{\varepsilon,\delta}_{N_{2}}\right\|_{L^{2}_{x}}.

Applying these bounds, we can estimate (
3.38
) by

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\sum_{N_{1}\sim N}\sum_{N_{2}\lesssim N}(N_{2}/N_{1})^{\frac{1}{2}}\int_{0}^{T}\|\nabla u^{\varepsilon,\delta}_{N_{1}}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\|\nabla u^{\varepsilon,\delta}_{N_{2}}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}dt

\displaystyle+\sum_{N_{2}\sim N}\sum_{N_{1}\lesssim N}(N_{1}/N_{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}\int_{0}^{T}\|\nabla u^{\varepsilon,\delta}_{N_{1}}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\|\nabla u^{\varepsilon,\delta}_{N_{2}}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}dt

\displaystyle+\sum_{N_{1}\gtrsim N}\sum_{N_{2}\sim N_{1}}(N/N_{1})^{2}\int_{0}^{T}\|\nabla u^{\varepsilon,\delta}_{N_{1}}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\|\nabla u^{\varepsilon,\delta}_{N_{2}}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}dt.

Performing the
N
summation first and then using Cauchy-Schwartz’s inequality, one can bound this by

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\sum_{N_{1}\sim N}\sum_{N_{2}\lesssim N}(N_{2}/N_{1})^{\frac{1}{2}}a_{N_{1}}a_{N_{2}}+\sum_{N_{2}\sim N}\sum_{N_{1}\lesssim N}(N_{1}/N_{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}a_{N_{1}}a_{N_{2}}

\displaystyle+\sum_{N_{1}\gtrsim N}\sum_{N_{2}\sim N_{1}}(N/N_{1})^{2}a_{N_{1}}a_{N_{2}},

where

\displaystyle a_{N}:=\|\nabla u^{\varepsilon,\delta}_{N}\|_{L^{2}((0,t)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x})}^{2}.

But form (
3.27
) and Bessel’s inequality, we have

\displaystyle\left\|m_{2}\right\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\lesssim\|\nabla u^{\varepsilon,\delta}\|_{L^{2}((0,t)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x})}^{2}.

(3.39)

Finally, by interpolation inequality for
L^{p}
-norms, (
3.27
), (
3.37
) and (
3.39
), we derive

\displaystyle\left\|m_{2}\right\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{p}_{x}}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\left\|m_{2}\right\|^{\frac{3}{2p}}_{L^{\frac{3}{2}}_{x}}\left\|m_{2}\right\|^{\frac{2p-3}{2p}}_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\right\|_{L^{1}_{t}}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|m_{2}\right\|^{\frac{3}{2p}}_{L^{1}_{t}L^{\frac{3}{2}}_{x}}\left\|m_{2}\right\|^{\frac{2p-3}{2p}}_{L^{1}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\|f_{in}\|_{L^{\infty}_{xv}(\mathbb{R}^{6})},\|f_{in}\|_{L^{1}_{xv}(\mathbb{R}^{6})},\left\||v|^{2}f_{in}\right\|_{L^{1}_{xv}(\mathbb{R}^{6})},\|u_{in}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\right)\left[(t+1)^{3}e^{3t}\right]

for any
p\in(\frac{3}{2},+\infty)
. To sum up, it becomes

\left\|m_{2}\right\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{p}_{x}}\leq C,~p\in\left[\frac{3}{2},+\infty\right].

For
m_{3}
, applying Lemma
A.2
we get

\displaystyle\|m_{3}\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{p}_{x}}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\int^{t}_{0}\left\|e^{(t-\tau)\triangle}\mathbb{P}\left(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}vf^{\varepsilon,\delta}dv\right)\right\|_{L^{p}_{x}}d\tau\right\|_{L^{1}_{t}}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\int^{t}_{0}(t-\tau)^{\frac{3-2p}{2p}}\left\|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}vf^{\varepsilon,\delta}dv\right\|_{L^{\frac{3}{2}}_{x}}d\tau\right\|_{L^{1}_{t}}

\displaystyle\leq Ct^{\frac{3}{2p}}\left\|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}|v|f^{\varepsilon,\delta}dv\right\|_{L^{\frac{3}{2}}_{x}L^{1}_{t}},

and by (
2.19
), (
2.22
) and (
3.29
), this becomes

\displaystyle\leq Ct^{\frac{3}{2p}}\left\|f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right\|^{\frac{1}{3}}_{L^{\infty}_{txv}}\left\||v|^{3}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right\|^{\frac{2}{3}}_{L^{1}_{txv}}

\displaystyle\leq C(t+1)^{3}e^{C(t+1)^{3}e^{4t}},

where

C=C\left(\|f_{in}\|_{L^{1}_{xv}},\|f_{in}\|_{L^{\infty}_{xv}},\left\||v|^{3}f_{in}\right\|_{L^{1}_{xv}},\|u_{in}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\right)
and
p\in\left[\frac{3}{2},+\infty\right)
.

Following the same methodology as in the estimate of
m_{3}
, we derive
m_{4}
:

\displaystyle\|m_{4}\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{p}_{x}}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\int^{t}_{0}\left\|e^{(t-\tau)\triangle}\mathbb{P}\left(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}dv\right)\right\|_{L^{p}_{x}}d\tau\right\|_{L^{1}_{t}}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\int^{t}_{0}(t-\tau)^{\frac{3-2p}{2p}}\left\|\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}dv\right\|_{L^{\frac{3}{2}}_{x}}d\tau\right\|_{L^{1}_{t}}

\displaystyle\leq Ct^{\frac{3}{2p}}\left\|\left\|u^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right\|_{L^{6}_{x}}\left\|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}dv\right\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\right\|_{L^{1}_{t}}.

By (
2.19
), (
2.22
), (
3.27
) and (
3.29
), this becomes

\displaystyle\leq Ct^{\frac{3+p}{2p}}\left\|u^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{6}_{x}}\left\|f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{\infty}_{txv}}\left\||v|^{3}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{1}_{xv}}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\|f_{in}\|_{L^{1}_{xv}},\|f_{in}\|_{L^{\infty}_{xv}},\left\||v|^{3}f_{in}\right\|_{L^{1}_{xv}},\|u_{in}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\right)(t+1)^{3}e^{C(t+1)^{4}e^{4t}},

where
p\in\left[\frac{3}{2},+\infty\right)
. To sum up, we prove (
3.36
).

MM

\square

Using the above lemma, we then obtain the estimate on
|v|^{\kappa}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}
.

Lemma 3.7
.

Under the assumptions of Theorem
1.4
and by Lemma
3.6
, we have

\displaystyle\left\||v|^{\kappa}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(0,t;L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}))}

(3.40)

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\|u_{in}\|_{L^{2}_{x}},\left\||v|^{\kappa}f_{in}\right\|_{L^{1}_{xv}},\left\|f_{in}\right\|_{L^{1}_{xv}},\left\|f_{in}\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{xv}}\right)(t+1)^{\frac{3\kappa+12}{2}}e^{C(\kappa)(t+1)^{\frac{14}{5}}e^{4t}},

where
\kappa\geq 3
.

Proof.

Firstly, multiply
|v|^{\kappa}
on both sides of the equation
\eqref{eq:NSVFK0}_{2}
:

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{xv}^{6}}|v|^{\kappa}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}dvdx=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}_{xv}^{6}}[(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta}-v\gamma_{\delta}(v))f^{\varepsilon,\delta}-\nabla_{v}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}]\cdot\nabla_{v}|v|^{\kappa}dvdx

(3.41)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C({\kappa})\int_{\mathbb{R}_{x}^{3}}|\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta}|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}|v|^{{\kappa}-1}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}dvdx+C({\kappa})\int_{\mathbb{R}_{xv}^{6}}|v|^{\kappa}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}dvdx

\displaystyle+C({\kappa})\int_{\mathbb{R}_{xv}^{6}}|v|^{{\kappa}-2}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}dvdx.

By Young’s inequality, this becomes

\displaystyle\leq C({\kappa})\int_{\mathbb{R}_{x}^{3}}|\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta}|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}|v|^{\kappa-1}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}dvdx+C({\kappa})\int_{\mathbb{R}_{xv}^{6}}|v|^{\kappa}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}dvdx+C({\kappa})\int_{\mathbb{R}_{xv}^{6}}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}dvdx,

and using (
2.19
) and Hölder’s inequality, it can be controlled by

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C({\kappa})\left\|f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{xv}}^{\frac{1}{3+\kappa}}\|u^{\varepsilon,\delta}\|_{L^{\kappa+3}_{x}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}_{xv}^{6}}|v|^{\kappa}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}dvdx\right)^{\frac{2+\kappa}{3+\kappa}}

\displaystyle+C({\kappa})\int_{\mathbb{R}_{xv}^{6}}|v|^{\kappa}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}dvdx+C({\kappa})\int_{\mathbb{R}_{xv}^{6}}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}dvdx.

Then, integrating (
3.41
) with respect to
t
and by (
1.12
), this yields

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}_{xv}^{6}}|v|^{\kappa}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}(t,\cdot)dvdx

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}_{xv}^{6}}|v|^{\kappa}f_{in}dvdx+C({\kappa})\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{xv}^{6}}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}dvdxds+C({\kappa})\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{xv}^{6}}|v|^{\kappa}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}dvdxds

\displaystyle+C({\kappa})\left\|f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{txv}}^{\frac{1}{3+\kappa}}\int_{0}^{t}\|u^{\varepsilon,\delta}\|_{L^{\kappa+3}_{x}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}_{xv}^{6}}|v|^{\kappa}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}dvdx\right)^{\frac{2+\kappa}{3+\kappa}}ds.

By (
2.22
) and the integral form of Grönwall’s inequality (Lemma
A.5
), we derive

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{xv}^{6}}|v|^{\kappa}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}(t,\cdot)dxdv\leq\left(C_{1}^{\frac{1}{3+\kappa}}\exp[C({\kappa})t]+C({\kappa})\exp[C({\kappa})t]\int_{0}^{t}\|u^{\varepsilon,\delta}\|_{L^{\kappa+3}_{x}}(s)ds\right)^{\kappa+3},

which
C_{1}=\int_{\mathbb{R}_{xv}^{6}}|v|^{\kappa}f_{in}dvdx+C({\kappa})t\int_{\mathbb{R}_{xv}^{6}}f_{in}dvdx
.
Finally, (
3.40
) is proved by Lemma
3.6
.

MM

\square

3.3.
The estimate of
P^{\varepsilon,\delta}

Lemma 3.8
.

Under the initial conditions prescribed in Theorem
1.4

, we have

\displaystyle\left\|P^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right\|_{L^{\frac{5}{3}}((0,t)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x})}\leq C\left((t+1)^{3}e^{5t}\right)^{\frac{3}{5}},

(3.42)

where

\displaystyle C=C\left(\|f_{in}\|_{L^{\infty}_{xv}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3})},\|f_{in}\|_{L^{1}_{xv}(\mathbb{R}^{6})},\left\||v|^{2}f_{in}\right\|_{L^{1}_{xv}(\mathbb{R}^{6})},\|u_{in}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\right).

Proof.

By the equation of
u^{\varepsilon,\delta}
, we have

\displaystyle-\Delta P^{\varepsilon,\delta}=\partial_{i}\partial_{j}((\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta}_{i})u^{\varepsilon,\delta}_{j})+\nabla\cdot\left[\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}(v-\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta})f^{\varepsilon,\delta}dv\right].

By Calderón-Zygmund theorem, Lemma
A.10
and Lemma
A.11
, it shows that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}_{x}^{3}}|P^{\varepsilon,\delta}|^{\frac{5}{3}}dx

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{\mathbb{R}_{x}^{3}}|(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta})\otimes u^{\varepsilon,\delta}|^{\frac{5}{3}}dx

\displaystyle\hskip 85.35826pt+C\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}_{x}^{3}}\left|\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}(v-\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta})f^{\varepsilon,\delta}dv\right|^{\frac{15}{14}}dx\right)^{\frac{14}{15}}.

Integrating both sides of the inequality with respect of
t
from 0 to
t
, this becomes

\displaystyle\int_{(0,t)\times\mathbb{R}_{x}^{3}}|P^{\varepsilon,\delta}|^{\frac{5}{3}}dxdt

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{(0,t)\times\mathbb{R}_{x}^{3}}|(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta})\otimes u^{\varepsilon,\delta}|^{\frac{5}{3}}dxdt

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+C\int^{t}_{0}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}_{x}^{3}}\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}(v-\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta})f^{\varepsilon,\delta}dv\right|^{\frac{15}{14}}dx\right)^{\frac{14}{15}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}|\theta_{\varepsilon}|dxdt

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{(0,t)\times\mathbb{R}_{x}^{3}}|(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta})\otimes u^{\varepsilon,\delta}|^{\frac{5}{3}}dxdt

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+C\int^{t}_{0}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}_{x}^{3}}\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}(v-\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta})f^{\varepsilon,\delta}dv\right|^{\frac{15}{14}}dx\right)^{\frac{14}{15}}dt

\displaystyle:=l_{1}+l_{2}.

For
l_{1}
, using Hölder’s inequality, Young’s inequality and the interpolation inequality, we have

\displaystyle l_{1}

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{(0,t)\times\mathbb{R}_{x}^{3}}|u^{\varepsilon,\delta}|^{\frac{10}{3}}dxdt

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\|u^{\varepsilon,\delta}\|_{L^{\infty}\left(0,t;L^{2}{(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x})}\right)}+\|\nabla u^{\varepsilon,\delta}\|_{L^{2}\left(0,t;L^{2}{(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x})}\right)}\right)^{\frac{10}{3}}.

For
l_{2}
, by Minkowski’s inequality, it follows that

\displaystyle l_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\int^{t}_{0}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}_{x}^{3}}\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}_{v}^{3}}(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta})f^{\varepsilon,\delta}dv\right|^{\frac{15}{14}}dx\right)^{\frac{14}{15}}dt

\displaystyle\hskip 142.26378pt+C\int^{t}_{0}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}_{x}^{3}}\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}_{v}^{3}}vf^{\varepsilon,\delta}dv\right|^{\frac{15}{14}}dx\right)^{\frac{14}{15}}dt

\displaystyle:=l_{21}+l_{22}.

For
l_{21}
, by (
2.19
), Hölder’s inequality, Young’s convolution inequality, (
3.27
) and (
3.34
), this yields

\displaystyle l_{21}

\displaystyle\leq C\int^{t}_{0}\left\||\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta}|\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}dv\right|\right\|_{L_{x}^{\frac{15}{14}}}dt

\displaystyle\leq C\int^{t}_{0}\|\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta}\|_{L_{x}^{\frac{10}{3}}}\left\|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}dv\right\|_{L_{x}^{\frac{30}{19}}}dt

\displaystyle\leq C\int^{t}_{0}\|u^{\varepsilon,\delta}\|_{L_{x}^{\frac{10}{3}}}\left\|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|v|^{\frac{33}{19}}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}dv\right\|_{L_{x}^{1}}^{\frac{19}{30}}\|f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\|_{L_{xv}^{\infty}}^{\frac{11}{30}}dt

\displaystyle\leq Ct^{\frac{7}{10}}\|u^{\varepsilon,\delta}\|_{L_{tx}^{\frac{10}{3}}}\left\|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(1+|v|^{2})f^{\varepsilon,\delta}dv\right\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}L_{x}^{1}}^{\frac{19}{30}}\|f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\|_{L_{txv}^{\infty}}^{\frac{11}{30}}

\displaystyle\leq Ct^{\frac{7}{10}}(\|u^{\varepsilon,\delta}\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}L_{x}^{2}}+\|\nabla u^{\varepsilon,\delta}\|_{L_{t}^{2}L_{x}^{2}})\left\|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(1+|v|^{2})f^{\varepsilon,\delta}dv\right\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}L_{x}^{1}}^{\frac{19}{30}}\|f^{\varepsilon.\delta}\|_{L_{txv}^{\infty}}^{\frac{11}{30}}.

For
l_{22}
, similar to the proof in
l_{21}
, by (
2.19
) we derive

\displaystyle l_{22}

\displaystyle\leq C\int^{t}_{0}\left\|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|v|f^{\varepsilon,\delta}dv\right\|_{L_{x}^{\frac{15}{14}}}dt

\displaystyle\leq C\int^{t}_{0}\left\|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|v|^{\frac{9}{7}}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}dv\right\|_{L_{x}^{1}}^{\frac{14}{15}}\|f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\|_{L_{xv}^{\infty}}^{\frac{1}{15}}dt

\displaystyle\leq Ct\|f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\|_{L_{txv}^{\infty}}^{\frac{1}{15}}\left\|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(1+|v|^{2})f^{\varepsilon,\delta}dv\right\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}L_{x}^{1}}^{\frac{14}{15}}.

Thus, by (
3.27
) it holds that

\displaystyle\int_{(0,t)\times\mathbb{R}_{x}^{3}}\left|P^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right|^{\frac{5}{3}}dxds\leq C(t+1)^{3}e^{5t},

where

\displaystyle C=C\left(\|f_{in}\|_{L^{\infty}_{xv}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3})},\|f_{in}\|_{L^{1}_{xv}(\mathbb{R}^{6})},\left\||v|^{2}f_{in}\right\|_{L^{1}_{xv}(\mathbb{R}^{6})},\|u_{in}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\right).

MM

\square

4.
Global energy inequality and local energy inequality

In this section, we establish a global energy inequality and two local energy inequalities of weak type for the regularized system, which play an important role in the proof of suitable weak solutions.

4.1.
Construction of global energy inequality

Lemma 4.1
.

Under the assumptions of Theorem
1.4
, assume that
(u^{\varepsilon,\delta},f^{\varepsilon,\delta})
is a regular solution of (
1
) in
(0,T)\times\mathbb{R}_{x}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{v}^{3}
. Then the following inequality holds:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}|u^{\varepsilon,\delta}(t,\cdot)|^{2}dx+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{6}_{xv}}\left(\frac{1}{2}|v|^{2}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}+f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\log f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right)(t,\cdot)dxdv

(4.43)

\displaystyle+\int_{(0,t)\times\mathbb{R}_{x}^{3}}|\nabla u^{\varepsilon,\delta}|^{2}dxds+\int_{(0,t)\times\mathbb{R}_{xv}^{6}}\frac{|(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta}-v)f^{\varepsilon,\delta}-\nabla_{v}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}|^{2}}{f^{\varepsilon,\delta}}dxdvds

\displaystyle\leq\int_{(0,t)\times\mathbb{R}_{xv}^{6}}|v|^{2}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}(1-\gamma_{\delta}(v))dxdvds

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{6}_{xv}}\left(\frac{1}{2}|v|^{2}f_{in}+f_{in}^{\delta}\log f_{in}^{\delta}\right)dxdv+\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}|u_{in}|^{2}dx.

Proof.

Firstly, multiplying
u^{\varepsilon,\delta}
on the first equation of (
1
) and integrating over
\mathbb{R}^{3}
yields:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|u^{\varepsilon,\delta}|^{2}dx+\|\nabla u^{\varepsilon,\delta}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{6}}\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta}\cdot(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta}-v)f^{\varepsilon,\delta}dxdv=0.

(4.44)

Combining (
3.33
) and (
4.44
), we obtain (
4.43
) and the proof is complete.

MM

\square

4.2.
Construction of local energy inequality of the first type

Lemma 4.2
.

Let
\Omega_{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}
and
\Omega_{v}\in\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}
be arbitrary bounded domains and
\phi
and
\psi
be cut-off functions, which vanish on the parabolic boundary of
(0,t)\times\Omega_{x}\times\Omega_{v}
and
(0,t)\times\Omega_{x}
.
Under the assumptions of Theorem
1.4
, assume that
(u^{\varepsilon,\delta},f^{\varepsilon,\delta})
is a regular solution of (
1
) in
(0,t)\times\Omega_{x}\times\Omega_{v}\subset\mathbb{R}^{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}
and
(0,t)\times\Omega_{x}\subset\mathbb{R}^{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}
. Then the following inequality holds:

\begin{split}&\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega_{x}}\left(|u^{\varepsilon,\delta}|^{2}\psi\right)(t,\cdot)dx+\int_{\Omega_{x}\times\Omega_{v}}\left(\left(\frac{1}{2}|v|^{2}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}+f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\log f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right)\phi\right)(t,\cdot)dxdv\\
&+\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega_{x}}|\nabla u^{\varepsilon,\delta}|^{2}\psi dxds+\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega_{x}\times\Omega_{v}}\frac{|(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta}-v)f^{\varepsilon,\delta}-\nabla_{v}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}|^{2}}{f^{\varepsilon,\delta}}\phi dxdvds\\
\leq&\frac{1}{2}\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega_{x}}|u^{\varepsilon,\delta}|^{2}(\partial_{t}\psi+\Delta_{x}\psi)dxds+\frac{1}{2}\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega_{x}}|u^{\varepsilon,\delta}|^{2}(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta})\cdot\nabla_{x}\psi dxds\\
&+\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega_{x}\times\Omega_{v}}\left(\frac{|v|^{2}}{2}+\log f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right)f^{\varepsilon,\delta}(\partial_{t}\phi+\Delta_{v}\phi)dxdvds\\
&+\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega_{x}\times\Omega_{v}}\left(\frac{|v|^{2}}{2}+\log f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right)f^{\varepsilon,\delta}v\cdot\left(\nabla_{x}\phi-\nabla_{v}\phi\right)dxdvds\\
&+\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega_{x}}(P^{\varepsilon,\delta}-\bar{P}^{\varepsilon,\delta})u^{\varepsilon,\delta}\cdot\nabla_{x}\psi dxds-\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega_{x}\times\Omega_{v}}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}v\cdot(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta})\phi dxdvds\\
&+\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}v\cdot\left(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast(u^{\varepsilon,\delta}\psi)\right)dxdvds\\
&+\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega_{x}\times\Omega_{v}}\left(2+\frac{|v|^{2}}{2}+\log f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right)f^{\varepsilon,\delta}(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta})\cdot\nabla_{v}\phi dxdvds\\
&+\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}|\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta}|^{2}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\psi-(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta})\cdot\left(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast(u^{\varepsilon,\delta}\psi)\right)f^{\varepsilon,\delta}dxdvds\\
&+\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega_{x}\times\Omega_{v}}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}|v|^{2}(1-\gamma_{\delta}(v))\phi dxdvds\\
&+\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega_{x}\times\Omega_{v}}\left(\frac{|v|^{2}}{2}+\log f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right)f^{\varepsilon,\delta}(1-\gamma_{\delta}(v))v\cdot\nabla_{v}\phi dxdvds.\end{split}

(4.45)

Proof.

Firstly, multiplying
u^{\varepsilon,\delta}\psi
on the first equation of (
1
) and integrating over
\mathbb{R}^{3}
yields:

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega_{x}}\partial_{t}u^{\varepsilon,\delta}\cdot u^{\varepsilon,\delta}\psi dx+

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega_{x}}(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta})\cdot\nabla u^{\varepsilon,\delta}\cdot u^{\varepsilon,\delta}\psi dx-\int_{\Omega_{x}}\Delta u^{\varepsilon,\delta}\cdot u^{\varepsilon,\delta}\psi dx

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega_{x}}\nabla_{x}P^{\varepsilon,\delta}\cdot u^{\varepsilon,\delta}\psi dx-\int_{\Omega_{x}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}(v-\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta})\cdot\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast(u^{\varepsilon,\delta}\psi)dvdx

\displaystyle:=F_{1}+F_{2}+F_{3}+F_{4}+F_{5}=0,

where

\displaystyle F_{1}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega_{x}}\psi\partial_{t}|u^{\varepsilon,\delta}|^{2}dx

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\partial_{t}\int_{\Omega_{x}}|u^{\varepsilon,\delta}|^{2}\psi dx-\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega_{x}}|u^{\varepsilon,\delta}|^{2}\partial_{t}\psi dx;

\displaystyle F_{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega_{x}}\psi(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta})\cdot\nabla|u^{\varepsilon,\delta}|^{2}dx

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega_{x}}|u^{\varepsilon,\delta}|^{2}(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta})\cdot\nabla_{x}\psi dx

\displaystyle F_{3}

\displaystyle=\int_{\Omega_{x}}|\nabla u^{\varepsilon,\delta}|^{2}\psi dx+\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega_{x}}\nabla|u^{\varepsilon,\delta}|^{2}\nabla_{x}\psi dx

\displaystyle=\int_{\Omega_{x}}|\nabla u^{\varepsilon,\delta}|^{2}\psi dx-\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega_{x}}|u^{\varepsilon,\delta}|^{2}\Delta_{x}\psi dx;

\displaystyle F_{4}

\displaystyle=-\int_{\Omega_{x}}P^{\varepsilon,\delta}u^{\varepsilon,\delta}\cdot\nabla_{x}\psi dx

\displaystyle=-\int_{\Omega_{x}}(P^{\varepsilon,\delta}-\bar{P}^{\varepsilon,\delta})u^{\varepsilon,\delta}\cdot\nabla_{x}\psi dx;

and

\displaystyle F_{5}

\displaystyle=-\int_{\Omega_{x}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}v\cdot(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast(u^{\varepsilon,\delta}\psi))dvdx+\int_{\Omega_{x}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta})\cdot(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast(u^{\varepsilon,\delta}\psi))dvdx.

To sum up, we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\partial_{t}\int_{\Omega_{x}}

\displaystyle|u^{\varepsilon,\delta}|^{2}\psi dx+\int_{\Omega_{x}}|\nabla u^{\varepsilon,\delta}|^{2}\psi dx

(4.46)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega_{x}}|u^{\varepsilon,\delta}|^{2}\partial_{t}\psi dx+\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega_{x}}|u^{\varepsilon,\delta}|^{2}(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta})\cdot\nabla_{x}\psi dx

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega_{x}}|u^{\varepsilon,\delta}|^{2}\Delta_{x}\psi dx+\int_{\Omega_{x}}(P^{\varepsilon,\delta}-\bar{P}^{\varepsilon,\delta})u^{\varepsilon,\delta}\cdot\nabla_{x}\psi dx

\displaystyle+\int_{\Omega_{x}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}v\cdot(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast(u^{\varepsilon,\delta}\psi))dvdx-\int_{\Omega_{x}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta})\cdot(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast(u^{\varepsilon,\delta}\psi))dvdx.

Then, multiplying
\left(1+\frac{|v|^{2}}{2}+\log f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right)\phi
on the second equation of (
1
) and integrating over
\mathbb{R}^{6}
yields:

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega_{x}\times\Omega_{v}}

\displaystyle\partial_{t}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\left(1+\frac{|v|^{2}}{2}+\log f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right)\phi dxdv

\displaystyle+\int_{\Omega_{x}\times\Omega_{v}}(v\cdot\nabla_{x})f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\left(1+\frac{|v|^{2}}{2}+\log f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right)\phi dxdv

\displaystyle+\int_{\Omega_{x}\times\Omega_{v}}\nabla_{v}\cdot\left[\left(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta}-v\gamma_{\delta}(v)\right)f^{\varepsilon,\delta}-\nabla_{v}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right]\left(1+\frac{|v|^{2}}{2}+\log f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right)\phi dxdv

\displaystyle:=G_{1}+G_{2}+G_{3}=0,

where

\displaystyle G_{1}=

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\int_{\Omega_{x}\times\Omega_{v}}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\left(1+\frac{|v|^{2}}{2}+\log f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right)\phi dxdv

\displaystyle-\int_{\Omega_{x}\times\Omega_{v}}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\left(1+\frac{|v|^{2}}{2}+\log f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right)\partial_{t}\phi dxdv

\displaystyle-\int_{\Omega_{x}\times\Omega_{v}}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\partial_{t}\left(1+\frac{|v|^{2}}{2}+\log f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right)\phi dxdv

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\int_{\Omega_{x}\times\Omega_{v}}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\left(\frac{|v|^{2}}{2}+\log f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right)\phi dxdv-\int_{\Omega_{x}\times\Omega_{v}}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\left(\frac{|v|^{2}}{2}+\log f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right)\partial_{t}\phi dxdv;

\displaystyle G_{2}=

\displaystyle-\int_{\Omega_{x}\times\Omega_{v}}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\left(1+\frac{|v|^{2}}{2}+\log f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right)v\cdot\nabla_{x}\phi dxdv-\int_{\Omega_{x}\times\Omega_{v}}\phi v\cdot\nabla_{x}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}dxdv

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\int_{\Omega_{x}\times\Omega_{v}}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\left(\frac{|v|^{2}}{2}+\log f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right)v\cdot\nabla_{x}\phi dxdv;

and

\displaystyle G_{3}=

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega_{x}\times\Omega_{v}}\left[\left(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta}-v\gamma_{\delta}(v)\right)f^{\varepsilon,\delta}-\nabla_{v}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right]\cdot\left(-v-\frac{\nabla_{v}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}}{f^{\varepsilon,\delta}}\right)\phi dxdv

\displaystyle-\int_{\Omega_{x}\times\Omega_{v}}\left(1+\frac{|v|^{2}}{2}+\log f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right)\left[\left(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta}-v\gamma_{\delta}(v)\right)f^{\varepsilon,\delta}-\nabla_{v}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right]\cdot\nabla_{v}\phi dxdv

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle G_{31}+G_{32}.

For
G_{31}
, we have

\displaystyle G_{31}=

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega_{x}\times\Omega_{v}}\left[(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta}-v)f^{\varepsilon,\delta}-\nabla_{v}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right]\cdot(-v-\frac{\nabla_{v}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}}{f^{\varepsilon,\delta}})\phi dxdv

\displaystyle+\int_{\Omega_{x}\times\Omega_{v}}(1-\gamma_{\delta}(v))f^{\varepsilon,\delta}v\cdot\left(-v-\frac{\nabla_{v}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}}{f^{\varepsilon,\delta}}\right)\phi dxdv

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega_{x}\times\Omega_{v}}\frac{|(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta}-v)f^{\varepsilon,\delta}-\nabla_{v}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}|^{2}}{f^{\varepsilon,\delta}}\phi dxdv-\int_{\Omega_{x}\times\Omega_{v}}\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta}\cdot(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta}-v)f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\phi dxdv

\displaystyle-\int_{\Omega_{x}\times\Omega_{v}}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta})\cdot\nabla_{v}\phi dxdv-\int_{\Omega_{x}\times\Omega_{v}}v\cdot(vf^{\varepsilon,\delta}+\nabla_{v}f^{\varepsilon,\delta})(1-\gamma_{\delta}(v))\phi dxdv

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega_{x}\times\Omega_{v}}\frac{|(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta}-v)f^{\varepsilon,\delta}-\nabla_{v}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}|^{2}}{f^{\varepsilon,\delta}}\phi dxdv-\int_{\Omega_{x}\times\Omega_{v}}\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta}\cdot(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta}-v)f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\phi dxdv

\displaystyle-\int_{\Omega_{x}\times\Omega_{v}}|v|^{2}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}(1-\gamma_{\delta}(v))\phi dxdv+\int_{\Omega_{x}\times\Omega_{v}}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}(1-\gamma_{\delta}(v))v\cdot\nabla_{v}\phi dxdv

\displaystyle-\int_{\Omega_{x}\times\Omega_{v}}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta})\cdot\nabla_{v}\phi dxdv.

For
G_{32}
, this becomes

\displaystyle G_{32}=

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega_{x}\times\Omega_{v}}\left(1+\frac{|v|^{2}}{2}+\log f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right)\left[(v-\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta})f^{\varepsilon,\delta}+\nabla_{v}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right]\cdot\nabla_{v}\phi dxdv

\displaystyle+\int_{\Omega_{x}\times\Omega_{v}}\left(1+\frac{|v|^{2}}{2}+\log f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right)f^{\varepsilon,\delta}(\gamma_{\delta}(v)-1)v\cdot\nabla_{v}\phi dxdv

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle G_{32-1}+G_{32-2};

where

\displaystyle G_{32-1}=

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega_{x}\times\Omega_{v}}\left(1+\frac{|v|^{2}}{2}+\log f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right)f^{\varepsilon,\delta}v\cdot\nabla_{v}\phi dxdv

\displaystyle-\int_{\Omega_{x}\times\Omega_{v}}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\left(v+\frac{\nabla_{v}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}}{f^{\varepsilon,\delta}}\right)\cdot\nabla_{v}\phi dxdv

\displaystyle-\int_{\Omega_{x}\times\Omega_{v}}\left(1+\frac{|v|^{2}}{2}+\log f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right)f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\Delta_{v}\phi dxdv

\displaystyle-\int_{\Omega_{x}\times\Omega_{v}}\left(1+\frac{|v|^{2}}{2}+\log f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right)f^{\varepsilon,\delta}(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta})\cdot\nabla_{v}\phi dxdv

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega_{x}\times\Omega_{v}}\left(\frac{|v|^{2}}{2}+\log f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right)f^{\varepsilon,\delta}v\cdot\nabla_{v}\phi dxdv

\displaystyle-\int_{\Omega_{x}\times\Omega_{v}}\left(\frac{|v|^{2}}{2}+\log f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right)f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\Delta_{v}\phi dxdv

\displaystyle-\int_{\Omega_{x}\times\Omega_{v}}\left(1+\frac{|v|^{2}}{2}+\log f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right)f^{\varepsilon,\delta}(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta})\cdot\nabla_{v}\phi dxdv.

To sum up, it follows that

\displaystyle\partial_{t}

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega_{x}\times\Omega_{v}}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\left(\frac{|v|^{2}}{2}+\log f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right)\phi dxdv+\int_{\Omega_{x}\times\Omega_{v}}\frac{|(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta}-v)f^{\varepsilon,\delta}-\nabla_{v}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}|^{2}}{f^{\varepsilon,\delta}}\phi dxdv

(4.47)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega_{x}\times\Omega_{v}}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\left(\frac{|v|^{2}}{2}+\log f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right)(\partial_{t}\phi+\Delta_{v}\phi)dxdv

\displaystyle+\int_{\Omega_{x}\times\Omega_{v}}\left(2+\frac{|v|^{2}}{2}+\log f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right)f^{\varepsilon,\delta}(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta})\cdot\nabla_{v}\phi dxdv

\displaystyle+\int_{\Omega_{x}\times\Omega_{v}}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\left(\frac{|v|^{2}}{2}+\log f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right)v\cdot(\nabla_{x}\phi-\nabla_{v}\phi)dxdv

\displaystyle+\int_{\Omega_{x}\times\Omega_{v}}\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta}\cdot(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta}-v)f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\phi dxdv

\displaystyle+\int_{\Omega_{x}\times\Omega_{v}}|v|^{2}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}(1-\gamma_{\delta}(v))\phi dxdv

\displaystyle+\int_{\Omega_{x}\times\Omega_{v}}\left(\frac{|v|^{2}}{2}+\log f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right)f^{\varepsilon,\delta}(1-\gamma_{\delta}(v))v\cdot\nabla_{v}\phi dxdv.

Combining (
4.46
) and (
4.47
), and integrating with respect to
t
, (
4.45
) is proved. Thus, the proof is complete.

MM

\square

4.3.
Construction of local energy inequality of the second type

Lemma 4.3
.

Let
\Omega_{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}
be arbitrarily bounded domains, and let
\psi
be cut-off functions that vanish on the parabolic boundary of
(0,t)\times\Omega_{x}
. Under the assumptions stated in Theorem
1.4
, assume
(u^{\varepsilon,\delta},f^{\varepsilon,\delta})
is a regular solution of (
1
) in
(0,t)\times\Omega_{x}\subset\mathbb{R}^{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}
and
(0,t)\times\Omega_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}\subset\mathbb{R}^{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}
. Then the following inequality holds:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega_{x}}\left(|u^{\varepsilon,\delta}|^{2}\psi\right)(t,\cdot)dx+\int_{\Omega_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\left(\left(\frac{1}{2}|v|^{2}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}+f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\log f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right)\psi\right)(t,\cdot)dxdv

\displaystyle+\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega_{x}}|\nabla u^{\varepsilon,\delta}|^{2}\psi dxds+\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\frac{|(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta}-v)f^{\varepsilon,\delta}-\nabla_{v}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}|^{2}}{f^{\varepsilon,\delta}}\psi dxdvds

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega_{x}}|u^{\varepsilon,\delta}|^{2}(\partial_{t}\psi+\Delta_{x}\psi)dxds

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega_{x}}|u^{\varepsilon,\delta}|^{2}(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta})\cdot\nabla_{x}\psi dxds+\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega_{x}}(P^{\varepsilon,\delta}-\bar{P}^{\varepsilon,\delta})u^{\varepsilon,\delta}\cdot\nabla_{x}\psi dxds

\displaystyle+\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}|\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta}|^{2}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\psi-(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta})\cdot\left(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast(u^{\varepsilon,\delta}\psi)\right)f^{\varepsilon,\delta}dxdvds

(4.48)

\displaystyle+\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}|v|^{2}(1-\gamma_{\delta}(v))\psi dxdvds

\displaystyle+\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\left(\frac{|v|^{2}}{2}+\log f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right)f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\partial_{t}\psi dxdvds

\displaystyle+\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\left(\frac{|v|^{2}}{2}+\log f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right)f^{\varepsilon,\delta}v\cdot\nabla_{x}\psi dxdvds

\displaystyle+\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}v\cdot\left(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast(u^{\varepsilon,\delta}\psi)\right)-f^{\varepsilon,\delta}v\cdot\left(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right)\psi dxdvds.

Proof.

Replacing
\phi
with
\psi
in (
4.47
) yields a new energy inequality. Combining this energy inequality with (
4.46
) implies (
4.3
).

MM

\square

5.
Convergence

5.1.
Weak convergence in
L^{p}
Spaces

Define
j^{\varepsilon,\delta}=\int_{\mathbb{R}_{v}^{3}}vf^{\varepsilon,\delta}dv,n^{\varepsilon,\delta}=\int_{\mathbb{R}_{v}^{3}}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}dv
. In this subsection, we prove weak convergence of
j^{\varepsilon,\delta}
,
n^{\varepsilon,\delta}
and

\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta}
in some
L^{p}
norms.
Before giving the proof, we recall a useful lemma regarding the issue of weak convergence for the product of two weakly convergent functions.

Proposition 5.1

(Lemma 5.1 in
[
47
]
)
.

Let
\varOmega
be
\mathbb{R}^{d}
or a bounded open domain with smooth boundary. Suppose
g^{n},h^{n}
converge weakly to
g,h
respectively in
L^{p_{1}}(0,T;L^{p_{2}}(\varOmega))
and
L^{q_{1}}(0,T;L^{q_{2}}(\varOmega))
where
(p_{1},p_{2}),(q_{1},q_{2})
are conjugate pairs, and
1\leq p_{i},q_{i}\leq\infty
. We assume that

\displaystyle\partial_{t}g^{n}~\text{bounded in}~L^{1}\left(0,T;W^{-1,1}(\varOmega)\right),

\displaystyle\|h^{n}-h^{n}(\cdot,\cdot+\xi)\|_{L^{q_{1}}(0,T;L^{q_{2}}(\varOmega))}\rightarrow 0\quad\text{as}~|\xi|\rightarrow 0,\text{uniformly in}~n.

Then
g^{n}h^{n}\rightharpoonup gh
(in the sense of distribution uniformly on
\varOmega\times(0,T)
).

Motivated by Lemma 3.2 in
[
49
]
, we have the following estimates.

Lemma 5.2
.

Let
f
be a measurable function in region
[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}
. Assume further that
f
satisfies

\displaystyle\|f\|_{L^{\infty}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})}+\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{xv}^{6}}|v|^{2}fdxdv\leq M.

Then there exists a constant
C
, depending on
M
, such that

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|n(t)\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x})}

\displaystyle\leq C\quad\text{for}~p\in[1,\frac{5}{3}],

(5.49)

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|j(t)\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x})}

\displaystyle\leq C\quad\text{for}~p\in[1,\frac{5}{4}],

(5.50)

where

j=\int_{\mathbb{R}_{v}^{3}}vfdv,n=\int_{\mathbb{R}_{v}^{3}}fdv.

Proof.

Proof of (
5.49
).

Let
p,q\in(1,+\infty)
satisfying
\frac{1}{p}+\frac{1}{q}=1
, and we have

\displaystyle n(t,x)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}_{v}^{3}}(1+|v|)^{\frac{2}{p}}f^{\frac{1}{p}}\frac{f^{\frac{1}{q}}}{(1+|v|)^{\frac{2}{p}}}dv

\displaystyle\leq\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}_{v}^{3}}(1+|v|)^{2}fdv\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}_{v}^{3}}\frac{f}{(1+|v|)^{\frac{2q}{p}}}dv\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}.

In particular, if
\frac{2q}{p}>3
, we deduce

\displaystyle n(x,t)\leq C\|f\|_{L^{\infty}_{v}}^{\frac{1}{q}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}_{v}^{3}}(1+|v|)^{2}fdv\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}

and

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|n(t)\|_{L^{p}_{x}}^{p}\leq C\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{xv}^{6}}(1+|v|)^{2}fdvdx.

Then, note that the condition
\frac{2q}{p}>3
is equivalent to

\displaystyle p<\frac{5}{3}.

As
p=\frac{5}{3}
, it follows from Lemma
2.2
that

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|n(t)\|^{\frac{5}{3}}_{L^{\frac{5}{3}}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x})}\leq\left(\frac{4}{3}\pi\|f\|_{L^{\infty}_{txv}}+1\right)^{\frac{5}{3}}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{xv}^{6}}f|v|^{2}dxdv\leq C.

To sum up, we derive
p\in[1,\frac{5}{3}]
.

Proof of (
5.50
).
The same arguments imply

\displaystyle j(t,x)

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}_{v}^{3}}(1+|v|)^{\frac{2}{p}}f^{\frac{1}{p}}\frac{f^{\frac{1}{q}}}{(1+|v|)^{\frac{2}{p}-1}}dv

\displaystyle\leq\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}_{v}^{3}}(1+|v|)^{2}fdv\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}_{v}^{3}}\frac{f}{(1+|v|)^{\frac{2q}{p}-q}}dv\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}.

In particular, if
\frac{2q}{p}-q>3
, we deduce

\displaystyle j(x,t)\leq C\|f\|_{L^{\infty}_{v}}^{\frac{1}{q}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}_{v}^{3}}(1+|v|)^{2}fdv\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}

and

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|j(t)\|_{L^{p}_{x}}^{p}\leq C\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{xv}^{6}}(1+|v|)^{2}fdvdx.

Finally, note that the condition
\frac{2q}{p}-q>3
is equivalent to

\displaystyle p<\frac{5}{4}.

As
p=\frac{5}{4}
, it follows from Lemma
2.2
that

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|j(t)\|^{\frac{5}{4}}_{L^{\frac{5}{4}}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x})}\leq\left(\frac{4}{3}\pi\|f\|_{L^{\infty}_{txv}}+1\right)^{\frac{5}{4}}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{xv}^{6}}f|v|^{2}dxdv\leq C.

To sum up, we derive
p\in[1,\frac{5}{4}]
.

MM

\square

Proposition 5.3
.

Under the assumptions of Theorem
1.4
, the following weak convergence results hold:

\displaystyle j^{\varepsilon,\delta}u^{\varepsilon,\delta}

\displaystyle\rightharpoonup ju\quad\text{in}~L^{1}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}),

(5.51)

\displaystyle n^{\varepsilon,\delta}u^{\varepsilon,\delta}

\displaystyle\rightharpoonup nu\quad\text{in}~L^{1}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}),

(5.52)

where
j^{\varepsilon,\delta}=\int_{\mathbb{R}_{v}^{3}}vf^{\varepsilon,\delta}dv,n^{\varepsilon,\delta}=\int_{\mathbb{R}_{v}^{3}}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}dv
.

Proof.

Firstly, due to Lemma
5.2
, it follows that

\displaystyle n^{\varepsilon,\delta}\rightharpoonup n\quad L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{p}(\mathbb{R}_{x}^{3}))-\text{weak\textasteriskcentered}\quad\forall p\in[1,\frac{5}{3}],

\displaystyle j^{\varepsilon,\delta}\rightharpoonup j\quad L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{p}(\mathbb{R}_{x}^{3}))-\text{weak\textasteriskcentered}\quad\forall p\in[1,\frac{5}{4}],

as
\varepsilon,\delta\rightarrow 0
(up to a subsequence)
1
1

1

Next the statement of
\varepsilon,\delta\rightarrow 0
(taking at most one subsequence) is frequently used below. For the sake of simplicity and without causing confusion, we will omit this expression.
. Also, due to (
3.27
), we have

\displaystyle u^{\varepsilon,\delta}\rightharpoonup u\quad L^{2}(0,T;L^{p}(\mathbb{R}_{x}^{3}))-\text{weak}\quad\forall p\in[2,6].

(5.53)

Then, integrating the second equation of (
1
) with respect to
v
, we find
\partial_{t}n^{\varepsilon,\delta}=-div_{x}j^{\varepsilon,\delta}
. It shows that

\displaystyle\partial_{t}n^{\varepsilon,\delta}~\text{is bounded in}~L^{1}(0,T;W^{-1,1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x})).

Multiplying
v
on the second equation of (
1
) and integrating over
\mathbb{R}^{6}
yields

\displaystyle\partial_{t}j^{\varepsilon,\delta}=-\int_{\mathbb{R}_{v}^{3}}\left(v\cdot\nabla_{x}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right)vdv+3\int_{\mathbb{R}_{v}^{3}}\left(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta}-v\gamma_{\delta}(v)\right)f^{\varepsilon,\delta}dv.

For
\int_{\mathbb{R}_{v}^{3}}\left(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta}-v\gamma_{\delta}(v)\right)f^{\varepsilon,\delta}dv
, by (
3.27
), (
3.34
) and (
5.49
), this becomes

\displaystyle\int_{(0,T)\times\mathbb{R}_{xv}^{6}}

\displaystyle|\left(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta}-v\gamma_{\delta}(v)\right)f^{\varepsilon,\delta}|dxdvdt

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{(0,T)\times\mathbb{R}_{xv}^{6}}|\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta}|f^{\varepsilon,\delta}dvdxdt+\int_{(0,T)\times\mathbb{R}_{xv}^{6}}|v|f^{\varepsilon,\delta}dvdxdt

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle CT^{\frac{17}{20}}\|n^{\varepsilon,\delta}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{\frac{5}{3}}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{3}))}\|u^{\varepsilon,\delta}\|_{L^{\frac{20}{3}}(0,T;L^{\frac{5}{2}}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{3}))}+\frac{1}{2}\int_{(0,T)\times\mathbb{R}_{xv}^{6}}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}dvdxdt

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\int_{(0,T)\times\mathbb{R}_{xv}^{6}}|v|^{2}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}dvdxdt

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C(T).

By

\displaystyle-\int_{\mathbb{R}_{v}^{3}}\left(v\cdot\nabla_{x}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right)vdv\in L^{1}(0,T;W^{-1,1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x})),

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}_{v}^{3}}\left(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta}-v\gamma_{\delta}(v)\right)f^{\varepsilon,\delta}dv\in L^{1}(0,T;L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x})),

it shows that

\displaystyle\partial_{t}j^{\varepsilon,\delta}~\text{is bounded in}~L^{1}(0,T;W^{-1,1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x})).

Finally, since
\nabla u^{\varepsilon,\delta}
is bounded in
L^{2}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x})
, we derive

\displaystyle\|u^{\varepsilon,\delta}(t,x+\xi)-u^{\varepsilon,\delta}(t,x)\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{1}|\xi|\|\nabla u^{\varepsilon,\delta}(t,x+\tau\xi)\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}d\tau

\displaystyle\leq C|\xi|.

The interpolation inequality for
L^{p}
-norms yields

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\|u^{\varepsilon,\delta}(t,x+\xi)-u^{\varepsilon,\delta}(t,x)\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{p}_{x}}

\displaystyle\leq\|u^{\varepsilon,\delta}(t,x+\xi)-u^{\varepsilon,\delta}(t,x)\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}^{\frac{6-p}{2p}}\|u^{\varepsilon,\delta}(t,x+\xi)-u^{\varepsilon,\delta}(t,x)\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{6}_{x}}^{\frac{3p-6}{2p}}

\displaystyle\leq C(p)|\xi|^{\frac{6-p}{2p}},

where
p\in(2,6)
. As
\xi\rightarrow 0
, it follows that

\displaystyle\|u^{\varepsilon,\delta}(t,x+\xi)-u^{\varepsilon,\delta}(t,x)\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{p}_{x}}\rightarrow 0.

To sum up, the weak convergence of

\displaystyle j^{\varepsilon,\delta}u^{\varepsilon,\delta}

\displaystyle\rightharpoonup ju\quad\text{in}~L^{1}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}),

\displaystyle n^{\varepsilon,\delta}u^{\varepsilon,\delta}

\displaystyle\rightharpoonup nu\quad\text{in}~L^{1}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x})

is proved by Proposition
5.1
.

MM

\square

Proposition 5.4
.

According to the properties of convolution and weak convergence, the following weak convergence holds for all
p\in[2,6]
and
\tilde{p}\in[2,\frac{10}{3}]
:

\displaystyle\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta}\rightharpoonup u\quad

\displaystyle\text{weakly in }L^{2}(0,T;L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x})),

(5.54)

\displaystyle\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta}\rightharpoonup u\quad

\displaystyle\text{weakly in }L^{\tilde{p}}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}),

(5.55)

where
u
is the limit function of (
5.53
).

Proof.

For any
\psi\in L^{2}(0,T;L^{p^{\prime}}(\mathbb{R}_{x}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{v}^{3}))
, which
\frac{1}{p}+\frac{1}{p^{\prime}}=1
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{(0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta}-u)\psi dxds

\displaystyle=\int_{(0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta}-\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u)\psi dxds+\int_{(0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u-u)\psi dxds

\displaystyle=\int_{(0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}(u^{\varepsilon,\delta}-u)\psi dxds+\int_{(0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}(u^{\varepsilon,\delta}-u)(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast\psi-\psi)dxds

\displaystyle\hskip 227.62204pt+\int_{(0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u-u)\psi dxds

\displaystyle:=m_{11}+m_{12}+m_{13}.

For
m_{11}
, due to (
5.53
), we derive

\displaystyle\int_{(0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}(u^{\varepsilon,\delta}-u)\psi dxds\rightarrow 0,\quad\text{as}~\varepsilon,\delta\rightarrow 0.

For
m_{12}
and
m_{13}
, due to the properties of mollifiers, (
3.27
) and (
3.34
), we have

\displaystyle\bigg|\int_{(0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}

\displaystyle(u^{\varepsilon,\delta}-u)(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast\psi-\psi)dxds\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq\left(\|u^{\varepsilon,\delta}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}))}+\|u\|_{L^{2}(0,T;L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}))}\right)\|\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast\psi-\psi\|_{L^{2}(0,T;L^{p^{\prime}}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}))}\rightarrow 0,\quad\text{as}~\varepsilon\rightarrow 0.

and

\displaystyle\left|\int_{(0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u-u)\psi dxds\right|

\displaystyle\leq\int_{(0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left|(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u-u)\psi\right|dxds

\displaystyle\leq\|\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u-u\|_{L^{2}(0,T;L^{p}(\mathbb{R}_{x}^{3}))}\|\psi\|_{L^{2}(0,T;L^{p^{\prime}}(\mathbb{R}_{x}^{3}))}\rightarrow 0,\quad\text{as}~\varepsilon\rightarrow 0.

To sum up, the proof of (
5.54
) is completed. Due to the embedding inequality (
3.34
),
the following weak convergence holds

\displaystyle\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta}\rightharpoonup u\quad\text{weakly in }L^{\tilde{p}}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}).

MM

\square

Corollary 5.5
.

The similar arguments as in Proposition
5.3
, by noting Proposition
5.4
, yields that

\displaystyle j^{\varepsilon,\delta}(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta})

\displaystyle\rightharpoonup ju\quad\text{in}~L^{1}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}),

(5.56)

\displaystyle n^{\varepsilon,\delta}(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta})

\displaystyle\rightharpoonup nu\quad\text{in}~L^{1}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}).

(5.57)

5.2.
The strong convergence of
f^{\varepsilon,\delta}
in
L^{p}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})

In this subsection, we prove the strong convergence of
f^{\varepsilon,\delta}
in
L^{p}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})
, and the following lemma of DiPerna-Lions plays an important role.

Proposition 5.6

(
[
25
]
)
.

Let
h^{n}
be a bounded sequence in
L^{1}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{m})
satisfying

\displaystyle\sup_{n}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{|x|\geq R}|h^{n}|dxdt\rightarrow 0,~\text{as}~R\rightarrow\infty,

and let
g^{n}_{in}
be a bounded sequence in
L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{m})
satisfying

\displaystyle\sup_{n}\int_{|x|\geq R}|g^{n}_{in}|dx\rightarrow 0,~\text{as}~R\rightarrow\infty.

If
g^{n}
is the solution of

\displaystyle\partial_{t}g^{n}+Eg^{n}=h^{n}~\text{in}~(0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{m},g^{n}|_{t=0}=g^{n}_{in}~\text{in}~\mathbb{R}^{m},

which
E
is a second-order, elliptic, possibly degenerate operator on
\mathbb{R}^{m}
with smooth coefficients. The sequence
g^{n}
is compact in
L^{1}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{m})
.

Remark 5.7
.

As shown on pages 7 and 21 of
[
25
]
, the partial diffusive transport (hypoelliptic) operator

L_{v}\equiv\partial_{t}+v\cdot\nabla_{x}-\Delta_{v}

satisfies the conditions of Proposition
5.6
to hold.

It follows from Proposition
5.6
and Remark
5.7
that the following lemma holds:

Lemma 5.8
.

Let
\{f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\}
be the solution family of system (
1
) under the initial conditions in Theorem
1.4
. Then for any
1\leq p<\infty
,

\{f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\}\text{ is compact in }L^{p}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}).

Proof.

Firstly, due to the assumption of
f_{in}^{\delta}
, we have

\left\{\begin{aligned} \int_{\mathbb{R}_{v}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{x}^{3}}|v|^{3}f_{in}^{\delta}dxdv&\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}_{v}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{x}^{3}}|v|^{3}f_{in}dxdv,\\
\int_{\mathbb{R}_{v}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{x}^{3}}|x|^{2}f_{in}^{\delta}dxdv&\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}_{v}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{x}^{3}}|x|^{2}f_{in}dxdv,\end{aligned}\right.

and

\left\{\begin{aligned} \int_{\mathbb{R}_{x}^{3}}\int_{|v|\geq R}f_{in}^{\delta}dxdv&\leq R^{-3}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{x}^{3}}\int_{|v|\geq R}|v|^{3}f_{in}dvdx\rightarrow 0,\\
\int_{|x|\geq R}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{v}^{3}}f_{in}^{\delta}dxdv&\leq R^{-2}\int_{|x|\geq R}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{v}^{3}}|x|^{2}f_{in}dvdx\rightarrow 0,\ \end{aligned}\right.

as
R\rightarrow+\infty
, uniformly for
\delta
.
By the same arguments, combining (
3.29
) and (
3.30
), it follows that

\displaystyle\sup_{\varepsilon,\delta}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}\int_{|v|\geq R}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}dvdx\rightarrow 0,~\text{as}~R\rightarrow\infty;

(5.58)

\displaystyle\sup_{\varepsilon,\delta}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}\int_{|v|\geq R}|v|f^{\varepsilon,\delta}dvdx\rightarrow 0,~\text{as}~R\rightarrow\infty;

\displaystyle\sup_{\varepsilon,\delta}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}\int_{|v|\geq R}|v|^{2}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}dvdx\rightarrow 0,~\text{as}~R\rightarrow\infty.

(5.59)

Claim that

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla_{v}\cdot\left[\left(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta}-v\gamma_{\delta}(v)\right)f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right]\right\|_{L^{1}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})}\leq C,

(5.60)

\displaystyle\sup_{\varepsilon,\delta}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{x}^{3}}\int_{|v|\geq R}\left|\nabla_{v}\cdot\left[\left(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta}-v\gamma_{\delta}(v)\right)f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right]\right|dvdxds\rightarrow 0,~\text{as}~R~\rightarrow\infty,

(5.61)

and

\displaystyle\sup_{\varepsilon,\delta}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{|x|\geq R}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\left|\nabla_{v}\cdot\left[\left(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta}-v\gamma_{\delta}(v)\right)f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right]\right|dvdxds\rightarrow 0,~\text{as}~R~\rightarrow\infty.

(5.62)

Then, by Proposition
5.6
, there exists a subsequence such that

\displaystyle f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\rightarrow f\quad\text{strongly in }L^{1}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}).

By the interpolation inequality for
L^{p}
-norms combined with the uniform estimate (
2.22
), we obtain the strong convergence

f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\to f\quad\text{strongly in }L^{p}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})

(5.63)

for all
p\in[1,+\infty)
.

Proof of (
5.60
) and (
5.61
).

Considering the velocity divergence term

\nabla_{v}\cdot\left[\left(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta}-v\gamma_{\delta}(v)\right)f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right],

we obtain the following estimate for
|v|\geq R
:

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{x}^{3}}\int_{|v|\geq R}\left|\nabla_{v}\cdot\left[\left(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta}-v\gamma_{\delta}(v)\right)f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right]\right|dvdxds

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{x}^{3}}\int_{|v|\geq R}\left|(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta})\nabla_{v}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right|dvdxds

\displaystyle\quad+3\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{x}^{3}}\int_{|v|\geq R}\gamma_{\delta}(v)f^{\varepsilon,\delta}dvdxds

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{x}^{3}}\int_{|v|\geq R}\left|\gamma_{\delta}(v)v\cdot\nabla_{v}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right|dvdxds

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{x}^{3}}\int_{|v|\geq R}\left|f^{\varepsilon,\delta}v\cdot\nabla_{v}\gamma_{\delta}(v)\right|dvdxds

\displaystyle:=e_{1}+e_{2}+e_{3}+e_{4}.

For
e_{1}
, by Hölder’s inequality, (
2.17
), (
3.27
), (
3.32
) and (
3.34
), this becomes

\displaystyle e_{1}

\displaystyle=2\left\|\sqrt{f^{\varepsilon,\delta}}(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta})\cdot\nabla_{v}\sqrt{f^{\varepsilon,\delta}}\right\|_{L^{1}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\{|v|\geq R\})}

\displaystyle\leq C\left\|(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta})\left\|f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right\|_{L^{1}(|v|\geq R)}^{\frac{1}{2}}\left\|\nabla_{v}\sqrt{f^{\varepsilon,\delta}}\right\|_{L^{2}(|v|\geq R)}\right\|_{L^{1}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x})}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(1+\frac{4}{3}\pi\left\|f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{txv}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left\|(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta})\left\||v|^{2}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right\|_{L^{1}(|v|\geq R)}^{\frac{3}{10}}\left\|\nabla_{v}\sqrt{f^{\varepsilon,\delta}}\right\|_{L^{2}(|v|\geq R)}\right\|_{L^{1}_{tx}}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(1+\frac{4}{3}\pi\left\|f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{txv}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\|u^{\varepsilon,\delta}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{5}_{x}}\left\|\nabla_{v}\sqrt{f^{\varepsilon,\delta}}\right\|_{L^{2}_{txv}}\left\||v|^{2}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\{|v|\geq R\})}^{\frac{3}{10}}

\displaystyle\leq C\left\||v|^{2}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\{|v|\geq R\})}^{\frac{3}{10}},

where

\displaystyle C=C\left(T,\|f_{in}\|_{L^{\infty}_{xv}},\|f_{in}\|_{L^{1}_{xv}},\left\||v|^{2}f_{in}\right\|_{L^{1}_{xv}},\|u_{in}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}},\|f_{in}\log f_{in}\|_{L^{1}_{xv}},\left\||x|^{2}f_{in}\right\|_{L^{1}_{xv}}\right).

For
e_{2}
, it is similary with (
5.58
). We omit it.

For
e_{3}
, by Hölder’s inequality and (
3.32
), we have

\displaystyle e_{3}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{x}^{3}}\int_{|v|\geq R}\left|\gamma_{\delta}(v)\sqrt{f^{\varepsilon,\delta}}v\cdot\nabla_{v}\sqrt{f^{\varepsilon,\delta}}\right|dvdxds

\displaystyle\leq CT^{\frac{1}{2}}\left\|\nabla_{v}\sqrt{f^{\varepsilon,\delta}}\right\|_{L^{2}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})}\left\||v|^{2}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\{|v|\geq R\})}^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\left\||v|^{2}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\{|v|\geq R\})}^{\frac{1}{2}},

where

\displaystyle C=C\left(T,\|f_{in}\|_{L^{\infty}_{xv}},\|f_{in}\|_{L^{1}_{xv}},\left\||v|^{2}f_{in}\right\|_{L^{1}_{xv}},\|u_{in}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}},\|f_{in}\log f_{in}\|_{L^{1}_{xv}},\left\||x|^{2}f_{in}\right\|_{L^{1}_{xv}}\right).

For
e_{4}
, due to
|v\cdot\nabla_{v}\gamma|\leq C
and the positive of
f^{\varepsilon,\delta}
, we obtain

\displaystyle e_{4}\leq C\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{x}^{3}}\int_{|v|\geq R}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}dvdxds,

To sum up, (
5.61
) can be obtained by (
5.58
) and (
5.59
).
Moreover, by replacing
|v|\geq R
with
\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}
, (
5.60
) also can be obtained.

Proof of (
5.62
).

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{x}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{v}^{3}}|x|^{\frac{3}{20}}\left|\nabla_{v}\cdot\left[\left(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta}-v\gamma_{\delta}(v)\right)f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right]\right|dvdxds

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{x}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{v}^{3}}|x|^{\frac{3}{20}}\left|(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta})\nabla_{v}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right|dvdxds

\displaystyle\quad+3\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{x}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{v}^{3}}|x|^{\frac{3}{20}}\gamma_{\delta}(v)f^{\varepsilon,\delta}dvdxds

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{x}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{v}^{3}}|x|^{\frac{3}{20}}\left|\gamma_{\delta}(v)v\cdot\nabla_{v}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right|dvdxds

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{x}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{v}^{3}}|x|^{\frac{3}{20}}\left|v\cdot\nabla_{v}\gamma_{\delta}(v)f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right|dvdxds

\displaystyle:=O_{1}+O_{2}+O_{3}+O_{4}.

For
O_{1}
, by (
2.17
), (
3.27
) and (
3.32
), this becomes

\displaystyle O_{1}

\displaystyle=2\left\||x|^{\frac{3}{20}}\sqrt{f^{\varepsilon,\delta}}(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta})\cdot\nabla_{v}\sqrt{f^{\varepsilon,\delta}}\right\|_{L^{1}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}_{v}^{3})}

\displaystyle\leq C\left\||x|^{\frac{3}{20}}(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta})\left\|f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}_{v}^{3})}^{\frac{1}{2}}\left\|\nabla_{v}\sqrt{f^{\varepsilon,\delta}}\right\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{v}^{3})}\right\|_{L^{1}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x})}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(1+\frac{4}{3}\pi\left\|f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{txv}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left\||x|^{\frac{3}{20}}(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta})\left\||v|^{2}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}_{v}^{3})}^{\frac{3}{10}}\left\|\nabla_{v}\sqrt{f^{\varepsilon,\delta}}\right\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{v}^{3})}\right\|_{L^{1}_{tx}}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(1+\frac{4}{3}\pi\left\|f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{txv}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left\|(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta})\left\||x|^{\frac{1}{2}}|v|^{2}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}_{v}^{3})}^{\frac{3}{10}}\left\|\nabla_{v}\sqrt{f^{\varepsilon,\delta}}\right\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{v}^{3})}\right\|_{L^{1}_{tx}}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(1+\frac{4}{3}\pi\left\|f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{txv}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\|u^{\varepsilon,\delta}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{5}_{x}}\left\|\nabla_{v}\sqrt{f^{\varepsilon,\delta}}\right\|_{L^{2}_{txv}}\left\||x|^{\frac{1}{2}}|v|^{2}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}_{v}^{3})}^{\frac{3}{10}}

\displaystyle\leq C\left\||x|^{\frac{3}{2}}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}_{v}^{3})}^{\frac{1}{10}}\left\||v|^{3}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}_{v}^{3})}^{\frac{1}{5}},

where

\displaystyle C=C\left(T,\|f_{in}\|_{L^{\infty}_{xv}},\|f_{in}\|_{L^{1}_{xv}},\left\||v|^{3}f_{in}\right\|_{L^{1}_{xv}},\|u_{in}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}},\|f_{in}\log f_{in}\|_{L^{1}_{xv}},\left\||x|^{2}f_{in}\right\|_{L^{1}_{xv}}\right).

Due to (
3.27
), (
3.29
) and (
3.30
), it follows that

\displaystyle O_{1}\leq C,

where

\displaystyle C=C\left(T,\|f_{in}\|_{L^{\infty}_{xv}},\|f_{in}\|_{L^{1}_{xv}},\left\||v|^{3}f_{in}\right\|_{L^{1}_{xv}},\|u_{in}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}},\|f_{in}\log f_{in}\|_{L^{1}_{xv}},\left\||x|^{2}f_{in}\right\|_{L^{1}_{xv}}\right).

For
O_{3}
, by Hölder’s inequality and (
3.32
), we have

\displaystyle O_{3}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{x}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{v}^{3}}|x|^{\frac{3}{20}}\left|\gamma_{\delta}(v)\sqrt{f^{\varepsilon,\delta}}v\cdot\nabla_{v}\sqrt{f^{\varepsilon,\delta}}\right|dvdxds

\displaystyle\leq C\left\|\nabla_{v}\sqrt{f^{\varepsilon,\delta}}\right\|_{L^{2}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})}\left\||x|^{\frac{3}{10}}|v|^{2}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right\|_{L^{1}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}_{v}^{3})}^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq CT^{\frac{1}{2}}\left\||x|^{\frac{9}{10}}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}_{v}^{3})}^{\frac{1}{6}}\left\||v|^{3}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}_{v}^{3})}^{\frac{1}{3}},

where

\displaystyle C=C\left(T,\|f_{in}\|_{L^{\infty}_{xv}},\|f_{in}\|_{L^{1}_{xv}},\left\||v|^{2}f_{in}\right\|_{L^{1}_{xv}},\|u_{in}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}},\|f_{in}\log f_{in}\|_{L^{1}_{xv}},\left\||x|^{2}f_{in}\right\|_{L^{1}_{xv}}\right).

Due to (
3.27
), (
3.29
) and (
3.30
), it follows that

\displaystyle O_{3}\leq C,

where

\displaystyle C=C\left(T,\|f_{in}\|_{L^{\infty}_{xv}},\|f_{in}\|_{L^{1}_{xv}},\left\||v|^{3}f_{in}\right\|_{L^{1}_{xv}},\|u_{in}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}},\|f_{in}\log f_{in}\|_{L^{1}_{xv}},\left\||x|^{2}f_{in}\right\|_{L^{1}_{xv}}\right).

For
O_{2}
and
O_{4}
, due to
|v\cdot\nabla_{v}\gamma|\leq C
and the positive of
f^{\varepsilon,\delta}
, we obtain

\displaystyle O_{2}+O_{4}\leq C\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{x}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{v}^{3}}|x|^{\frac{3}{20}}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}dvdxds.

Due to (
3.27
) and (
3.30
), we derive

\displaystyle O_{2}+O_{4}\leq C,

where

\displaystyle C=C\left(T,\|f_{in}\|_{L^{1}_{xv}},\left\||x|^{2}f_{in}\right\|_{L^{1}_{xv}}\right).

The following estimate holds for
|x|\geq R
:

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{|x|\geq R}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\left|\nabla_{v}\cdot\left[\left(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta}-v\gamma_{\delta}(v)\right)f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right]\right|dvdxds

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{|x|\geq R}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}|x|^{\frac{3}{20}}|x|^{-\frac{3}{20}}\left|\nabla_{v}\cdot\left[\left(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta}-v\gamma_{\delta}(v)\right)f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right]\right|dvdxds

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle R^{-\frac{3}{20}}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{|x|\geq R}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}|x|^{\frac{3}{20}}\left|\nabla_{v}\cdot\left[\left(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta}-v\gamma_{\delta}(v)\right)f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right]\right|dvdxds

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle CR^{-\frac{3}{20}}

which implies that

\displaystyle\sup_{\varepsilon,\delta}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{|x|\geq R}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\left|\nabla_{v}\cdot\left[\left(\theta_{\varepsilon}\ast u^{\varepsilon,\delta}-v\gamma_{\delta}(v)\right)f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\right]\right|dvdxds\rightarrow 0,~\text{as}~R~\rightarrow\infty.

The proof is complete.

MM

\square

5.3.
The strong convergence of
|v|^{\kappa}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}
in
L^{1}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})

It follows from Lemma
5.8
that the strong convergence of
|v|^{\kappa}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}
in
L^{1}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})
still holds.

Lemma 5.9
.

Under the assumptions stated in Theorem
1.4
, we have the strong convergence

|v|^{p}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\to|v|^{p}f\quad\text{strongly in }L^{1}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}),\quad 0\leq p<\kappa,

(5.64)

as
\varepsilon,\delta\rightarrow 0.

Proof.

By Hölder’s inequality and Lemma
3.7
, it shows that

\displaystyle\left\||v|^{p}(f^{\varepsilon,\delta}-f)\right\|_{L^{1}_{txv}}

\displaystyle\leq\left\||v|^{\kappa}\left|f^{\varepsilon,\delta}-f\right|\right\|^{\frac{p}{\kappa}}_{L^{1}_{txv}}\left\|f^{\varepsilon,\delta}-f\right\|^{\frac{\kappa-p}{\kappa}}_{L^{1}_{txv}}

\displaystyle\leq C\left\|f^{\varepsilon,\delta}-f\right\|^{\frac{\kappa-p}{\kappa}}_{L^{1}_{txv}}.

Hence, the proof is completed by Lemma
5.8
.

MM

\square

Corollary 5.10
.

Under the assumptions stated in Theorem
1.4
, the following strong convergence holds:

|v|^{p}f^{\varepsilon,\delta}\to|v|^{p}f\quad\text{strongly in }L^{q}(0,T;L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}))

for all exponents
p\in[0,\kappa)
and
q\in[1,+\infty)
.

Proof.

By an interpolation inequality for
L^{p}
norms, it follows that

\displaystyle\left\||v|^{p}\left(f^{\varepsilon,\delta}-f\right)\right\|_{{L^{q}_{t}L^{1}_{xv}}}\leq\left\||v|^{p}\left(f^{\varepsilon,\delta}-f\right)\right\|^{\frac{q-1}{q}}_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{1}_{xv}}\left\||v|^{p}\left(f^{\varepsilon,\delta}-f\right)\right\|_{L^{1}_{txv}}^{\frac{1}{q}},\quad\forall~q\in[1,+\infty).

(5.65)

By Lemma
3.27
, Lemma
3.7
and Lemma
5.9
, we derive

\displaystyle\left\||v|^{p}\left(f^{\varepsilon,\delta}-f\right)\right\|_{{L^{q}_{t}L^{1}_{xv}}}\rightarrow 0,

where
q\in[1,+\infty)
.

MM

\square

5.4.
The strong convergence of
u^{\varepsilon,\delta}
in
L^{2}(0,T;L^{q}(\Omega_{x}))

In this subsection, we consider the strong convergence of
