\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega_{x}}|u|^{2}u\cdot\nabla_{x}\psi dxds+\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\left(\frac{|v|^{2}}{2}+\log f\right)f\partial_{t}\psi dxdvds

\displaystyle+\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\left(\frac{|v|^{2}}{2}+\log f\right)fv\cdot\nabla_{x}\psi dxdvds+\int_{(0,t)\times\Omega_{x}}(P-\bar{P})u\cdot\nabla_{x}\psi dxds.

9.
Partial regularity

Before we start the main content, recall (
6.74
) and by the properties of weak convergence, we have the following:

\displaystyle\|f(t,x,v)\|_{L^{\infty}(0,t;L^{1}_{xv}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}))}+\|f(t,x,v)\|_{L^{\infty}((0,t)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\|u\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}(0,t;L^{2}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{3}))}

(9.84)

\displaystyle+\left\||v|^{\kappa}f\right\|_{L^{\infty}(0,t;{L^{1}_{xv}(\mathbb{R}^{6})})}+\|\nabla u\|^{2}_{L^{2}(0,t;{L^{2}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{3})})}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\|f_{in}\|_{L^{\infty}_{xv}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3})},\|f_{in}\|_{L^{1}_{xv}(\mathbb{R}^{6})},\left\||v|^{\kappa}f_{in}\right\|_{L^{1}_{xv}(\mathbb{R}^{6})},\|u_{in}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\right)(t+1)^{\frac{3\kappa+12}{2}}e^{C(\kappa)(t+1)^{3}e^{6t}}.

Next we prove Theorem
1.5
and Theorem
1.6
.

9.1.
The proof of Theorem
1.5

As in the proof of Lemma
3.7
, multiply
|v|^{15+\varkappa}

\left(\varkappa>0\right)
on both sides of (
1
) and integrate with
xv
. By Grönwall’s inequality, (
1.13
) and (
9.84
), we derive

\displaystyle\left\||v|^{15+\varkappa}f\right\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}))}\leq C.

(9.85)

For
\eqref{eq:NSVFK}_{1}
, the external force term of the Navier-Stokes equations is
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}(v-u)fdv
. Here we define
\varepsilon_{1}=\frac{5\varkappa}{144+6\varkappa}
. By (
2.17
) we derive

\displaystyle\left\|u\int_{\mathbb{R}_{v}^{3}}fdv\right\|_{L^{\frac{5}{2}+\varepsilon_{1}}_{tx}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\left(1+\frac{4}{3}\pi\|f\|_{L^{\infty}_{txv}}\right)\left\|u\left(\int f|v|^{15+\frac{144\varepsilon_{1}}{5-6\varepsilon_{1}}}\,dv\right)^{\frac{5-6\varepsilon_{1}}{30+12\varepsilon_{1}}}\right\|_{L^{\frac{5}{2}+\varepsilon_{1}}_{tx}},

and by Hölder’s inequality, it is controlled by

\displaystyle\left\|u\int_{\mathbb{R}_{v}^{3}}fdv\right\|_{L^{\frac{5}{2}+\varepsilon_{1}}_{tx}}\leq C\left(1+\frac{4}{3}\pi\|f\|_{L^{\infty}_{txv}}\right)\left\|f|v|^{15+\frac{144\varepsilon_{1}}{5-6\varepsilon_{1}}}\right\|^{\frac{5-6\varepsilon_{1}}{30+12\varepsilon_{1}}}_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{1}_{xv}}\|u\|_{L_{t}^{\frac{5}{2}+\varepsilon_{1}}L_{x}^{\frac{16}{7+6\varepsilon_{1}}+2}}.

By (
3.34
),
(
9.84
) and (
9.85
), we derive

\displaystyle\left\|u\int_{\mathbb{R}_{v}^{3}}fdv\right\|_{L^{\frac{5}{2}+\varepsilon_{1}}_{tx}}\leq C.

For the term of
\int vfdv
, by (
2.17
) and Hölder’s inequality, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}fvdv\right\|_{L^{\frac{5}{2}+\varepsilon_{1}}_{tx}}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(1+\frac{4}{3}\pi\|f\|_{L^{\infty}_{txv}}\right)\left\|\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}f|v|^{7+4\varepsilon_{1}}dv\right)^{\frac{2}{5+2\varepsilon_{1}}}\right\|_{L^{\frac{5+2\varepsilon_{1}}{2}}_{tx}}

\displaystyle\leq Ct^{\frac{2}{5+2\varepsilon_{1}}}\left(1+\frac{4}{3}\pi\|f\|_{L^{\infty}_{txv}}\right)\left\|f|v|^{7+4\varepsilon_{1}}\right\|^{\frac{2}{5+2\varepsilon_{1}}}_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{1}_{xv}}

\displaystyle\leq Ct^{\frac{2}{5+2\varepsilon_{1}}}\left(1+\frac{4}{3}\pi\|f\|_{L^{\infty}_{txv}}\right)\left(\left\|f|v|^{15+\frac{144\varepsilon_{1}}{5-6\varepsilon_{1}}}\right\|^{\frac{1}{p}}_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{1}_{xv}}\left\|f\right\|^{1-\frac{1}{p}}_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{1}_{xv}}\right)^{\frac{2}{5+2\varepsilon_{1}}}

\displaystyle\leq C(t),

where
p=\frac{75+54\varepsilon_{1}}{(5-6\varepsilon_{1})(7+4\varepsilon_{1})}
.
In summary, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\int(v-u)fdv\right\|_{L^{\frac{5}{2}+\varepsilon_{1}}_{tx}}\leq C.

By Lemma
A.7
and Lemma
A.8
, Theorem
1.5
is proved. ∎

9.2.
The proof of Theorem
1.6

Since
z_{0}
is a regular point of
u
, according to the definition of regular points, we have

\displaystyle\|u\|_{L^{\infty}_{tx}\left(Q(z_{0},r)\right)}\leq\Lambda

for some
r>0
, which
\Lambda
is a constant. Then for
\eqref{eq:NSVFK}_{2}
, we transform it into

\displaystyle\partial_{t}f+(v\cdot\nabla_{x})f-\Delta_{v}f=(v-u)\cdot\nabla_{v}f+3f.

Applying Theorem 1.2 in
[
69
]
(see also Theorem 1.4 in
[
32
]
), the proof is completed. ∎

Appendix A

Lemma A.1
.

(Lemma 3.7 in
[
65
]
)

Let
X\subset\subset Y\subset Z
be reflexive Banach space, if
0<T<\infty,p\geq 1,r>1,f\in L^{p}(0,T;X)
satisfy

\displaystyle\|f\|_{L^{p}(0,T;X)}\leq C_{1},\quad\|\partial_{t}f\|_{L^{r}(0,T;Z)}\leq C_{2}

for some constants
C_{1},C_{2}<\infty
, then
f_{j},j\in\mathbb{N}
is relatively compact in
L^{p}(0,T;Y)
.

Lemma A.2
.

(Proposition A.16 in
[
5
]
)
The following a priori estimate holds for
1\leq q\leq p\leq\infty
and
0\leq s\leq r<\infty
:

\displaystyle\left\|e^{\nu t\bigtriangleup}f\right\|_{\dot{W}^{r,p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\leq C(r,s)(\nu t)^{\frac{n}{2}(\frac{1}{p}-\frac{1}{q})-\frac{r-s}{2}}\left\|f\right\|_{\dot{W}^{s,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})},

as soon as
t>0
.

Lemma A.3
.

(Theorem A.1 in
[
5
]
)
Let
X
be a Banach space, let
B\subset X
be a closed bounded subset, and let
\Phi:B\rightarrow B
be Lipschitz continuous in the norm topology with Lipschitz constant
L<1
;
i.e.
,
\left\|\Phi(x)-\Phi(y)\right\|\leq L\left\|x-y\right\|
and
L<1
. Then
\Phi
has a unique fixed point in
B
.

Lemma A.4
.

(Appendix B.2 in
[
29
]
)
Let
\eta(\cdot)
be a non-negative absolutely continuous function on
[0,T]
, which satisfies for a.e.
t
the differential inequality

\displaystyle\eta^{{}^{\prime}}(t)\leq\phi(t)\eta(t)+\psi(t),

where
\phi(t)
and
\psi(t)
are non-negative summable functions on
[0,T]
. Then

\displaystyle\eta(t)\leq e^{\int_{0}^{t}\phi(s)ds}\left[\eta(0)+\int_{0}^{t}\psi(s)ds\right]

for all
0\leq t\leq T
.

Lemma A.5
.

(Theorem 21 in
[
26
]
)
Let
u(t)
be a nonnegative function that satisfies the integral inequality

\displaystyle u(t)\leq c+\int_{t_{0}}^{t}(a(s)u(s)+b(s)u^{\alpha}(s))ds,c\geq 0,\alpha\geq 0,

where
a(t)
and
b(t)
are continuous nonnegative functions for
t\geq t_{0}
. For
0\leq\alpha<1
we have

\displaystyle u(t)\leq\left\{c^{1-\alpha}\exp\left[(1-\alpha)\int_{t_{0}}^{t}a(s)ds\right]+(1-\alpha)\int_{t_{0}}^{t}b(s)\exp\left[(1-\alpha)\int_{s}^{t}a(r)dr\right]ds\right\}^{\frac{1}{1-\alpha}};

for
\alpha=1
,

\displaystyle u(t)\leq c\exp\left\{\int_{t_{0}}^{t}\left[a(s)+b(s)\right]ds\right\}.

Lemma A.6
.

( Corollary 4.5.5 in
[
6
]
)
Let
\mu
be a measure with values in
[0,+\infty]
and let functions
f_{n},f\in\mathcal{L}^{1}(\mu)
be such that
f_{n}(x)\rightarrow f(x)
a.e. Then convergence of
\{f_{n}\}
to
f
in
L^{1}(\mu)
is equivalent to the following:

\displaystyle\lim_{\mu(E)\rightarrow 0}\sup_{n}\int_{E}|f_{n}|d\mu=0

and, for every
\varepsilon>0
, there exists a measurable set
X_{\varepsilon}
such that
\mu(X_{\varepsilon})<\infty
and

\displaystyle\sup_{n}\int_{X/X_{\varepsilon}}|f_{n}|d\mu<\varepsilon.

Lemma A.7
.

(Theorem 6.2 in
[
65
]
)
Let
\Omega\subset\mathbb{R}^{3}
be any domain and let
0<T\leq\infty
. Let
(v,p)
be a suitable weak solution of (NS) in
\Omega_{T}=\Omega\times(0,T)
with force
f\in L^{q}(\Omega\times(0,T))
,
q>\frac{5}{2}
. Let the set of singular points of
v
be

S=\{z_{0}\in\Omega\times(0,T]:v\text{ is singular at }z_{0}\}.

Then
P^{1}(S)=0
.

Lemma A.8
.

(Theorem 6.3 in
[
65
]
)
Let
(v,p)
be a suitable weak solution of (NS) in
Q(z_{0},r_{0})\subset\mathbb{R}^{3+1}
,
r_{0}>0
, with force
f\in L^{q}(Q(z_{0},r_{0}))
,
q>\frac{5}{2}
. There is a small
\varepsilon_{0}=\varepsilon_{0}(q)>0
such that
v
is regular at
z_{0}
if

\limsup_{r\to 0+}\frac{1}{r}\iint_{Q(z_{0},r)}|\nabla v|^{2}<\varepsilon_{0}.

Lemma A.9
.

(Bernstein inequalities see Appendix A in
[
63
]
)

Let
d\geq 1
,
s\geq 0
, and
1\leq p\leq q\leq\infty
. Let
P_{\leq N}
,
P_{\geq N}
, and
P_{N}
denote the Littlewood–Paley projection operators as defined above. Then the following inequalities hold for all tempered distributions
f
for which the right-hand sides are finite:

(i)

(High frequency decay) For any
s\geq 0
,

\|P_{\geq N}f\|_{L_{x}^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim_{p,s,d}N^{-s}\|\,|\nabla|^{s}P_{\geq N}f\|_{L_{x}^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.

(ii)

(Derivative of low frequencies)

\|P_{\leq N}|\nabla|^{s}f\|_{L_{x}^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim_{p,s,d}N^{s}\|P_{\leq N}f\|_{L_{x}^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.

(iii)

(Characterization of homogeneous Sobolev norms on frequencies
\sim N
)

\|P_{N}|\nabla|^{\pm s}f\|_{L_{x}^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\sim_{p,s,d}N^{\pm s}\|P_{N}f\|_{L_{x}^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.

(iv)

(Low frequency Sobolev embedding)

\|P_{\leq N}f\|_{L_{x}^{q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim_{p,q,d}N^{\frac{d}{p}-\frac{d}{q}}\|P_{\leq N}f\|_{L_{x}^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.

(v)

(Single frequency Sobolev embedding)

\|P_{N}f\|_{L_{x}^{q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim_{p,q,d}N^{\frac{d}{p}-\frac{d}{q}}\|P_{N}f\|_{L_{x}^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.

The implicit constants in the inequalities
\lesssim
and
\sim
depend only on the subscripts.

Lemma A.10
.

(Riesz translation, Corollary A.12 in
[
5
]
)

Let
R_{i}=\partial_{x_{i}}(-\Delta)^{-1/2}
be the
i
th Riesz transform, or equivalently the Fourier multiplier with symbol
m(\xi)=i\xi_{i}|\xi|^{-1}
. Then
R_{i}
extends to a bounded operator
L^{p}\to L^{p}

\|R_{i}f\|_{L^{p}}\lesssim\|f\|_{L^{p}}

for
1<p<\infty
.

Lemma A.11
.

( Theorem 1 of Chapter 5 in
[
59
]
)
Assume
0<\alpha<n
,
1\leq p<\frac{n}{\alpha}
and,

I_{\alpha}f(x)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\frac{f(y)}{|x-y|^{n-\alpha}}dy.

When
p>1
and
\frac{1}{q}=\frac{1}{p}-\frac{\alpha}{n}
, there holds

\|I_{\alpha}f\|_{L^{q}}\leq C\|f\|_{L^{p}}.
